One backup runs at a time, and a second request joins it.

The engine starts the backups with the ledger (`server.usageLedger.start()`): the first check after 15 minutes (`BACKUP_FIRST_MS`: five minutes after the first ledger scan, so machines that restart together, as in an update, answer with their usage), then a check every 15 minutes (`BACKUP_CHECK_MS`), or at the backup time when it comes sooner. The timers are `unref`. A check makes a backup when the backups are on, the local time is at or after the backup time of today, and the folder has no backup file of this machine with the date of today. So a backup that the PC missed (off or asleep) runs at the first check later that day. A failed check logs its message only when it differs from the last logged message, and a good backup resets that message. **Back up now** logs nothing. A settings save starts the next check again with the same rule. The engine stop (`usage ledger save`) first stops the timers and waits only for a write that started; a backup that is still in its export writes nothing.

### Known X11 frame-rate limitation

No safe app-only 240 Hz fix was found on the tested stack. Keep Python and the current X11 workaround. Resize lag remains unresolved. See [the investigation report](docs/benchmarks/x11-frame-timing-2026-10-13.md) for methods, results, failed trials, and restoration checks.

The 2026-10-13 tests used ASB `1.7.0`, Debian `13`, GNOME/Mutter `48.7`, GTK `4.18.6`, Libadwaita `1.7.6`, NVIDIA `550.163.01` with RTX `4070 SUPER`, and GNOME X11. Monitors were primary landscape `1920×1080 @ 239.76 Hz` and portrait `1080×1920 @ 60 Hz`. All trials used 149 visible synthetic rows with unchanged UI/features.

The installed realize hook disables compositor-feedback frame synchronization. GTK reported `16667 µs` (about 60 Hz), consistent with [GTK's fallback](https://docs.gtk.org/gdk4/method.FrameClock.get_refresh_info.html). Restoring native synchronization on a fresh test surface gave these results from 105 adjacent frame intervals on the original layout:

| Interval | Median (ms) | 95th percentile (ms) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Paint | 0.703 | 1.778 |
| After-paint to `FRAME_DRAWN` receipt | 16.154 | 32.784 |
| Receipt to next paint | 0.036 | 0.083 |

The buffer held 214 protocol messages, with zero drops/errors. These are frame callbacks, not physical display FPS. The driver/Mutter/message-delivery split is unproven.

[Mutter's refresh selection](https://github.com/GNOME/mutter/blob/48.7/cogl/cogl/cogl-xlib-renderer.c#L419-L440) uses greatest overlap; equal-area ties follow output-name order. The 60 Hz portrait came first here. A temporary `900×1600` portrait mode changed reported refresh to `4171 µs` (about 240 Hz), but callbacks stayed near 33 ms (about 30 Hz). Default EGL, GLX with app-local VSync off, and GLX primary-device selection stayed near 33 ms. `GDK_DEBUG=no-vsync` did not improve changed-scroll cadence and used about 90% of one CPU core. Settings were restored. These trials support no permanent resolution or bypass-flag change.

After a distro, desktop, GTK, or driver change, record the exact stack, X11/Wayland, renderer/context, and monitor rates. Use the same synthetic data and window size on the high-rate monitor. Compare scroll, Drawer, resize, frame intervals, and CPU. Add a single-monitor versus mixed-monitor comparison, not yet done. Distinguish callbacks from display FPS. Verify all input, actions, and accessibility before removing the workaround. A distro change alone is not a proven fix.

## API and trust boundary

ASB makes no model calls and sends no messages. When ASB Bridge is installed, a message that it delivers starts a turn in the receiving app.

ASB uses `createAsbServer` from `src/asb-server.mjs`. It allows the view assets, dashboard, source events, app-source settings, agent status and UI reports, known-session open, ASB Read/Unread, Discard/Keep, drawer in/out, pins, pin movement, Persistent unread settings, and the window palette. Other API routes are not exposed.

Actions require the local Host and a matching Origin. App-source changes also need the app source token when the server has one (see below). Session IDs must be in the scanned list. Read/Unread, pin/unpin, and drawer in/out accept an empty JSON object. Persistent unread accepts only a `persistentUnread` boolean. Pin movement accepts `up`/`down`, or a known pinned target with `before`/`after`. Session actions accept no client commands, paths, or URLs.

Read APIs (dashboard, including forced refresh, sources, events, and agent status) accept an absent Origin or the exact local Origin. Sec-Fetch-Site must be absent, `same-origin`, or `none`. Cross-site reads return 403 before source loading. These access checks run before the `ETag` comparison of the dashboard. Native no-header requests and normal address-bar navigation remain supported; asset routes keep their existing behavior.

Malformed request URLs return 400 without stopping the server. A malformed session ID on an ASB state-action route gives 400. Malformed JSON gives 400 on every action route. The open reply has no `resumeCommand`. The `?refresh=` alias is not available; use `?force=1`. The dashboard reply has no `performance.notifications`. It has the counters `performance.dashboard.watchWalks` and `performance.dashboard.notModified`. The native HTTP client converts incomplete responses and bad status lines into its normal failure callback, including incomplete source-error bodies. Failure text does not include partial response data, and loading/action latches can recover.

| App-source route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/sources` | `revision`, registered sources, colors, compatibility profile metadata, chat counts/status, and the eight-source limit. Needs no token. |
| `POST /api/sources` | `{source: {...}}` with full `provider`, `label`, `dataDir`, `launcher`, and `enabled` fields. Include `id` to edit; omit it to add. Optional `color` uses `#RRGGBB`; optional `showMarker` is boolean. Claude can include `projectsDir` and `remoteClaude`. Needs the header `X-ASB-Source-Token` when a token is set. Returns `changed`, `savedRevision` (the revision of this request's write), and the current `revision`, `sources`, and `maxSources` read after the dashboard scan; another client's save during the scan can make `revision` differ from `savedRevision`. |
| `POST /api/sources/<id>/remove` | Empty JSON object; default source removal is rejected. Needs the header `X-ASB-Source-Token` when a token is set. Returns the same fields as `POST /api/sources`: `savedRevision` is this removal's write, and `revision` and `sources` are the current settings. |
| `POST /api/threads/<id>/discard-result` | Empty JSON object, or exactly `{"next": true}`; forces a source scan. With `{}`, it requires a known Working row and arms its current task once. With `{"next": true}`, a Working row arms its current task, an Idle row arms its next task, and any other state gets HTTP 400 `Discard requires a Working or Idle task.` Any other body gets HTTP 400 `Invalid ASB session action.` |
| `POST /api/threads/<id>/keep-result` | Empty JSON object; uses the normal cached lookup and clears a known row's Discard arm (current-task or next-task) without a forced scan. |
| `POST /api/threads/<id>/drawer-in` | Empty JSON object; same access checks as `mark-read`. Puts the row in the drawer. Gives 400 unless the row is unread with no question and no required action. Replies `{ changed, threadId, thread }`. |
| `POST /api/threads/<id>/drawer-out` | Empty JSON object; same access checks as `mark-read`. Takes the row out of the drawer. Gives 400 unless the row is in the drawer. Replies `{ changed, threadId, thread }`. |

| `POST /api/threads/<id>/mute` | Empty JSON object; same access checks as `mark-read`. Turns Mute on for a known row. Replies `{ changed, threadId, thread }` with `thread.muted: true`. |
| `POST /api/threads/<id>/unmute` | Empty JSON object; same access checks as `mark-read`. Turns Mute off for a known row. Replies `{ changed, threadId, thread }` with `thread.muted: false`. |

Each dashboard row has `surface` (`app` or `cli`), `machineId`, `machineName`, `remote`, `globalId`, and `remoteCache`. A Codex row is `cli` when its `source` is `cli`, `exec`, `terminal`, or `tui` (any case); a Claude Code CLI row is `cli`; Claude desktop, remote-cache, and OpenCode rows are `app`. `providerLabel` is `Codex`, `Codex CLI`, `Claude Desktop Code`, `Claude Code CLI`, or `OpenCode`. A row with `surface` `cli` or `remote: true` is view-only: `canOpen` is false and `appDeepLink` is `""`, and `openSwitchboardThread` refuses it with `This session cannot open from ASB.` `remote` is true only for rows from machine group members (see Machine group). While the engine is in a group, `machineName` of local rows is the group name of this machine. Else it is the host name before the first `.`, with only `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`, and `-`, at most 32 characters, else `This PC`. `machineId` is the first 16 characters of the SHA-256 fingerprint of the phone certificate. The engine creates the certificate at start with the gateway code, also when phone access is off, and passes the identity through the `machineIdentity` option of `createSwitchboardServer`. It reads the identity before the server listens, so the first scan already has it and no row with an empty `globalId` reaches a phone. Without a gateway (`npm start`) or a certificate (no `openssl`), `machineId` and `globalId` are `""`; else `globalId` is `<machineId>:<row id>`. `remoteCache` is true for rows from Claude's claude.ai cache. The ID is not logged and has no route.

Session references (`src/session-references.mjs`, the only grammar): the full form is `asb:1/<machine-id>/<source-id>/<scope-tag>/<type>:<app-session-id>`. It is printable ASCII with no space, at most 384 bytes, version exactly `1`, and exactly five segments. The machine ID is 16 lowercase hex characters, the source ID matches `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,79}`, and the scope tag is 32 lowercase hex characters. The types are `codex` (a hyphenated UUID), `claude-local` (`local_` and a UUID), `claude-cli` (a UUID), `claude-remote` (`cse_` or `session_` and 1 to 128 of `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`, `-`), and `opencode` (1 to 128 of the same characters). The app session ID is kept exactly, case included. A parse error has the code `invalid-reference` or `unsupported-reference-version`, and invalid full text is never read as the short form. A full reference identifies one checked observation: the record that ASB sees through one registered source. It does not say that this source owns or runs the session. `asb agent resolve-session` resolves a reference (see the `asb agent` paragraph). ASB Bridge `send_message` sends to one exact Codex, Claude Code CLI, or Claude Desktop Code session on this machine or on a member by its full reference (see the exact local sends and exact member sends paragraphs). Claude Code sessions use `asb agent resolve-session <reference>` and give its `Send name` value to native `SendMessage` (`sendName` in JSON). They never take an ID out of the reference. No `sendName` means send nothing. There is no copy control yet.

The server's `loadDashboard` wrapper adds these fields to each local row, before the machine group merge: `reference` (the full text) when the engine has a machine identity, the row's source is registered, and the app ID has a valid typed form; else `referenceUnavailable` (`no-machine-identity`, `unknown-source`, `invalid-app-id`, or `unsupported-type`). `referenceScope` is the scope tag of the row's source. `sessionReference` is the short text that the board copies today (`<sourceName or provider host>/<externalId>`), only when the ID has a valid form and the text is one printable line of at most 384 bytes. A remote-cache row uses this machine and the source that observes it, with the type `claude-remote`. When more than one source lists the same CLI session ID (two Claude sources with one registry folder), the kept row has a full reference only when exactly one of those listings has a transcript. Else the kept row never has a `referenceScope`. The usual reasons come first (for example `no-machine-identity`), and only a row that would get a `reference` gets `referenceUnavailable: 'ambiguous-source'` and no `reference` instead. Its `sessionReference` stays. The first displayed source is not evidence. Row `id`, `globalId`, pins, marks, state keys, and `sources.json` do not change. The `npm start` server has no machine identity, so its rows have `referenceUnavailable: 'no-machine-identity'`.

The scope tag guards the configured source location. Its input (`scopeInput`) is a JSON array of a domain label, the provider, the stored absolute `dataDir`, and for Claude the effective `projectsDir` (`~/.claude/projects` when it is not set; other providers use `""`). It uses the registry's stored paths only: no `stat`, `realpath`, inode, mtime, or file contents. The tag is the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA-256 of that input, as 32 lowercase hex characters. The key comes from HKDF-SHA-256 (info `asb session reference scope v1`) of the identity secret that the gateway already loads: the private key PEM bytes on Linux, the PFX passphrase on Windows (`referenceScopeTagger` and `referenceScopes()` in `src/phone-gateway.mjs`). The engine gets the tag function once after the machine identity and passes it with the `referenceScopes` option of `createSwitchboardServer`. The server makes the tags again only when the source revision or the function changes. The key stays in memory and is never logged or exported. A label rename keeps the tag. A `dataDir` or `projectsDir` edit, or a new machine identity, changes it. Limits: the tag guards the configured location, not the contents of a store. An in-place restore with the same native IDs, or a changed symlink target with the same configured path, gives the same tag. A move from A to B and back to the same A configuration gives A's tag again. A restored identical built-in source is the same scope. A removed added source usually gets a new source ID when you add it again.

Each dashboard row has the boolean fields `drawer` and `muted`. A drawer row has `unread: false`. The phone gateway does not forward `mute` or `unmute`; the phone and the web view show no Mute control. `mark-read` and `mark-unread` clear the drawer flag. The web view (`npm start`) shows a drawer row as read. It has no Drawer pill and no drawer actions.

Providers are `codex` and `claude-desktop-code`. Paths must be absolute local paths; additional launchers must be supported installed executables. Source updates reject raw command, argument, environment, and unknown fields. They use the same Host and Origin protection as session actions.

A local program that is not a browser can send Host and Origin itself, and a source change can set the launcher that ASB runs. For this reason, the engine creates one random token for each run and publishes it in `service.json`; the launcher writes it to the stdin of the window (one JSON line, with the URL), not to an argument, an environment variable, or a file. When a token is set, `POST /api/sources` and `POST /api/sources/<id>/remove` need the header `X-ASB-Source-Token`. Without it, the reply is 403 with `Use app source actions from the ASB window.` The native client sends the token on protected settings, palette, and UI report requests when it has one: from the line on stdin, or from `ASB_SOURCE_TOKEN` for a window that you start by hand with the URL as its argument. A server started with `npm start` has no token. Source writes then have only the Host and Origin checks; agent routes reject access.

ASB Bridge routes (`bridgeCoverage` of `src/bridge-coverage.mjs`). The engine runs `asb bridge status --json`, with `--claude-home` for the folder of each enabled Claude profile, in a child process (30 s limit). It starts a check only when a request asks, never on a timer, and runs one check at a time. A local result is kept for 60 s; a member value is kept for 60 s.

| Bridge route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/bridge` | Read route; needs no token; `Cache-Control: no-store`. `?refresh=1` starts a new check of this machine and asks each online member again. It waits at most 10 s for the checks. `{ api: 1, expectedVersion, machines }`, this machine first. Each machine is `{ machineId, name, isSelf, online, lastOkAtMs, asbVersion, state, checking, checkedAtMs, apps, live, error? }`. `state` is `ok`, `checking` (no result yet; `checking` is also true while a new check runs and an older value shows), `old` (the member has no bridge route; `live` is its session count from the message relay), `offline`, or `error` (with a sentence in `error`). Each app is `{ sourceId, label, provider, state, version, live, command, canInstall }` for one enabled profile. The app state is `ready`, `update` (an older plugin or skill), `hooks` (Codex hooks not trusted), `off` (turned off in Codex), `nobun` (a running Codex app-server cannot find Bun), `missing`, `conflict` (the old claude-uds-bridge plugin, or a bridge skill or plugin that was changed or is not from ASB), `old-app` (OpenCode older than 2.1.2), or `unknown` (no status for the Claude folder). `live` counts the live bridge receivers of the profile (Codex and OpenCode; `null` for Claude). `command` is the install command for a terminal of that machine, with home-relative paths, or `null` when a path is outside the home folder or needs quotes. `canInstall` is true only for this machine and the states `missing` and `update`. Members are asked with `GET /peer/v1/bridge` (4 s). |
| `POST /api/bridge/install` | Needs the source token (else 403). Body exactly `{ sourceId }` or `{ all: true }` (else 400). One install at a time (else 409 `An install is running. Wait for it to end.`). No chosen app with `canInstall` gives 404 `This profile has nothing to install.` It runs `asb bridge install` with `--codex-home <home>`, `--claude`, or `--opencode` (180 s limit); a Claude profile with its own folder runs `--claude` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to that folder. Then it checks again. `200 { ok, output }`: `ok` is true when every run ended with exit 0, and `output` is the CLI text, at most 4000 characters. |

The phone gateway never forwards these routes.

Activity routes (`src/activity-messages.mjs` and `src/activity-usage.mjs`, wired in `src/switchboard.mjs`). The two GET routes are read routes with the checks of `GET /api/dashboard` (Host, Origin, Sec-Fetch-Site), `Cache-Control: no-store`, and JSON. They read the bridge databases, the transcripts, the cached rows, and the member replies, and never write an app store. An unknown `period` gives 400 `{ "error": "Invalid period." }`; a read failure gives 500 `{ "error": "Cannot read the ASB activity." }`.

| Activity route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/activity/messages?period=24h\|7d\|30d[&text=0]` | Default `7d`. `{ period, nowMs, buckets, machines, seats, messages, truncated }`: one record per bridge message ID of kind `message` and per Claude Code message (ID `claude:<tool use ID>` or `claude:<record uuid>`), merged over the group, oldest first, at most the newest 20,000 (then `truncated: true`). A send and a receipt with the same text hash (of the trimmed body without the envelope) are one record when the receipt is at most 24 hours after the send, the send is at most 2 minutes after the receipt, and both have the same sender seat or one of the two sender seats is `unknown:`; the newest such send joins. A record has `id`, `from`, `to` (seat IDs: the dashboard row ID, or `unknown:<machine ID>` with the title `Other sessions`), `atMs`, the status group (`accepted`, `written`, `held`, `not-sent`, `unknown`; a receiver row wins), a fixed `reason` for `not-sent`, `local`, and `fromLabel` and `toLabel` (the name from the message for an `unknown:` seat, else `''`). A record has no `text`, and the engine reads no message text for this route: the texts come only with a thread. With `text=0` (the key once, with the value `0`; another value or a repeated key gives 400 `{ "error": "Invalid query." }`), the reply also leaves `fromLabel` and `toLabel` out. Each reply has `Cache-Control: no-store` and an `ETag`: the quoted base64url SHA-256 of its JSON without `nowMs`. An equal `If-None-Match` gets 304 with no body. A client that accepts gzip gets the JSON compressed with async gzip (`Content-Encoding: gzip`, `Vary: Accept-Encoding`); other clients get plain JSON. |
| `GET /api/activity/thread?period=24h\|7d\|30d&a=<seat>&b=<seat>` or `?period=…&seat=<seat>` | Default `7d`, the period rule of the messages route. Any other query key, a repeated key, an empty seat, a seat of more than 512 characters, or a seat ID with a control character gives 400 `{ "error": "Invalid session." }`. `no-store`. `{ total, messages }`: the newest 60 messages of the pair (both directions) or of the one session, oldest first, from the same merged records as the messages route; `total` counts all of them. A message is `{ id, from, to, atMs, status, reason, fromLabel, toLabel, text }`: the labels follow the messages route, and `text` is the body without the envelope, at most 2,000 characters, read for each of the 60 messages (the bridge row, the Claude transcript line, or the text that the member sent), also when the message is older than the newest 200 of the period; else `null`. The phone gateway never forwards it. |
| `GET /api/activity/usage?period=24h\|7d\|30d\|90d\|all` | Default `30d`. `{ period, nowMs, buckets, machines, records, prices, scan }`: `buckets.unit` is `hour`, `day`, or `month` (`all`: local month starts from the month of the oldest record of the report with more than 0 tokens at or after 1 January 2020 UTC (`FIRST_USAGE_MS`) through the current month). One record `{ machineId, app, model, maker, bucket, tokens, cost }` per machine, app, model, and bucket with the four token counts and the cost (`null` for an Unpriced model). `maker` is the company that makes the model (`modelMaker`); member replies do not send it. `prices` is `{ source: "LiteLLM", status, fetchedAtMs, models }` with the status `loading`, `fresh`, `cached`, or `unavailable`. `scan.complete` is false while the first scan runs, with `files` and `scannedFiles`. Each reply has `Cache-Control: no-store` and an `ETag`: the quoted base64url SHA-256 of its JSON without `nowMs`. An equal `If-None-Match` gets 304 with no body. A client that accepts gzip gets the JSON compressed with async gzip (`Content-Encoding: gzip`, `Vary: Accept-Encoding`); other clients get plain JSON. |
| `POST /api/engine/show-window` | Needs the source token (else 403). Body exactly `{ "window": "board" }`, `{ "window": "messages" }`, or `{ "window": "usage" }`, at most 1 KiB (else 400 `Invalid window request.`). For `board`, that Board calls `present()`. With no Board that reported in the last 45 s, it replies `{ "shown": false }`. Else it sends the `/api/events` event `show-window` with `{ window, clientId }`, where `clientId` is the Board that reported last, and replies `{ "shown": true }`. |

Usage ledger routes (`src/asb-server.mjs`; they exist only when the usage service has a ledger file, which the engine always gives): `GET /api/usage/export` is a read route with the checks of `GET /api/dashboard` that also needs the source token (else 403 `Use the current ASB source token to export usage.`), with `Cache-Control: no-store`. It runs a scan first, then replies with the export document `{ format: "asb-usage-export", version: 1, exportedAtMs, machines: [{ machineId, name, records }] }`: this machine (all hours, with no 50,000 limit; a name that is not a group name, such as `This PC`, goes out as `suggestName` makes it), each group member with records (its last reply merged with its imported records), then the imported machines, each machine once. A record is `[hourMs, app, model, uncached, cacheRead, cacheWrite, output]`, sorted by hour, app, and model, and only records that pass the import check go out. Without a machine ID it gives 409 `ASB has no machine ID yet. Start ASB again.`; another error gives 500 `ASB cannot export the usage.`. `POST /api/usage/import` is an action route that also needs the source token (else 403 `Use the current ASB source token to import usage.`). Its body is an export document of at most 64 MiB (`maxUsageImportBytes`, else 413 `The file is too large for an ASB usage export.`). The check is all or nothing (400 `This file is not an ASB usage export.`): exactly the four top-level keys, the format, version 1, a positive safe-integer `exportedAtMs`, and 1 to 64 machines with exactly `machineId` (16 lowercase hex characters), `name` (the group name rule), and `records` (at most 500,000). A record has exactly 7 items: an hour start from 1 January 2020 UTC to one day after now, an app of `[a-z][a-z0-9-]` (at most 64 characters), a model of 1 to 200 characters with no control character, and four safe integers of 0 or more. A repeated key in one file counts its larger value. The reply is `{ machines: [{ machineId, name, hours }] }`, where `hours` counts the different records of that machine in the file. When the ledger is not saved after the merge (a failed write, or a ledger that the engine cannot read), it gives 500 `ASB could not save the usage ledger. Keep the export file and try again.`; another error gives 500 `ASB cannot import the usage.`. The phone gateway never forwards these routes.

Usage backup routes (`src/asb-server.mjs`; they exist only when the usage service has backups, which the engine always gives): `GET /api/usage/backup` is a read route that also needs the source token (else 403 `Use the current ASB source token to read usage backups.`). It replies with exactly `{ enabled, time, keep, folder, path, defaultPath, nextAtMs, running, files, lastError }`: `folder` is the setting (`''` for the default), `path` the folder in use, and `defaultPath` the default folder; `nextAtMs` is `null` when the backups are off, else the backup time of today, or of tomorrow when today has a backup file (a time in the past means that the backup is due); `files` are the backup files of this machine, newest first, at most 100, each `{ name, date, bytes, modifiedAtMs }` in whole milliseconds; and `lastError` is `null` or `{ atMs, message }` of the last failed backup. Another error gives 500 `ASB cannot read the usage backups.` `POST /api/usage/backup/settings` is an action route that also needs the source token (else 403 `Use the current ASB source token to change usage backups.`). It takes exactly `{ enabled, time, keep, folder }` (at most 8 KiB): a bad body gives 400 `Invalid backup settings.`, a folder that is not absolute 400 `Use a full folder path.`, and a folder that does not exist 400 `This folder does not exist.`. A custom folder is saved after `path.normalize`. A failed save gives 500 `ASB cannot save the backup settings.`; else the reply is the status. `POST /api/usage/backup/now` is an action route that also needs the source token (else 403 `Use the current ASB source token to back up usage.`). It takes exactly `{}` (else 400 `Invalid backup request.`), makes a backup also when the backups are off, and replies with the status and `made: { name, path, machines, hours }`; a failure gives 409 or 500 with its message. All three send `Cache-Control: no-store`. The phone gateway never forwards these routes.

Top-bar route (`panelSummary` in `src/asb-server.mjs`, `panelMachines` in `src/switchboard.mjs`): `GET /api/panel` is a read route with the checks of `GET /api/dashboard`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and JSON. It exists only when the server gets `panelMachines`, and it reads the shared cached rows (no forced scan). `{ needs, working, total, needsRows, workingRows, machines, accent }`: the numbers of unarchived rows that are pending, that are Working, and all; at most 6 rows each, needs rows by `updatedAtMs` and working rows by `workingSinceMs` (newest first, then by ID), each `{ id, title, app, provider, kind, machine, atMs, canOpen }` (only needs rows have `kind`: `approval`, `question`, `failed`, or `done`; `machine` is the index in `machines`, or -1); the machines in the order of the Messages and Usage windows (this machine, then the members by name), each `{ name, self, online, total, needs, working }`; and `accent`, the accent of the saved board palette, or `''`. A failed load gives 500 `{ "error": "ASB cannot read the sessions." }`. The phone gateway and the member routes never forward it. Each `dashboard` event of a server with this route also has `panel`: the first 16 characters of the base64url SHA-256 of the JSON reply that `GET /api/panel` would give now (the cached rows, `panelMachines`, and the accent), so the tag changes exactly when the reply would change. The events keep their order while the server computes the tag.

Buckets are local hours (`24h`) or local calendar days, oldest first. The last hour starts at now minus the minutes, seconds, and milliseconds of the local time, so it is the current hour also in the hour that repeats when daylight saving time ends. The phone gateway forwards only the messages and usage reads (see Phone gateway), never the thread route. Neither the gateway nor the member routes reach `/api/engine/*`.

### Local agent interface

`asb agent status` reads runtime metadata and current revisions. `asb agent sources list`, `get <id|name>`, `add --file <json-file|->`, `update <id|name> --file <json-file|->`, and `remove <id|name>` manage sources through the running instance. An exact id wins; otherwise one source with that name is selected, and equal names give `source_ambiguous`. `asb agent peers` is read-only: it reads the source list from the running instance, then the owned `<pid>.json` records in each real Claude registry folder (`sessions` next to each Claude transcript folder, and `~/.claude/sessions`). It checks the process owner, state, and recorded start time before and after it reads `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `CODEX_HOME` from that process, and maps the agent home to exactly one source name or `unknown`. It reports only projected record fields and never connects to a peer socket. Each command supports `--json`. JSON input is at most 64 KiB, contains editable fields only, and rejects unknown fields. Update merges supplied fields into current settings. An equal update, a repeated Add for the same physical store with matching supplied settings, or Remove for an absent ID is unchanged. A different registration for an existing store requires Update. Writes use quoted `If-Match`; conflicts require a fresh read. See [the user guide](ASB.md#local-agent-commands).

`asb agent resolve-session <reference> [--source <source-id>] [--json]` always sends `{ reference, configDir }` or `{ reference, sourceId, configDir }` to the loopback action route `POST /api/agent/resolve-session` (exact Host, Origin `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, `Sec-Fetch-Site` `same-origin` or absent, the nonempty source token, a body of at most 8 KiB, `Cache-Control: no-store`). The route also accepts these bodies without `configDir`. This optional field must be an absolute path of at most 4096 characters. Another body gives 400 `Invalid resolve request.`. The route exists only on a `createSwitchboardServer` server; the `npm start` server has no source token, so it refuses the route with 403. The phone gateway and the member routes never forward it. The pure resolver is `resolveSessionReference` in `src/session-targets.mjs`, and `parseShortReference` in `src/session-references.mjs` is the short-form grammar (printable ASCII with no space, at most 384 bytes, one `/`, a host with the source ID rule, and an app ID of a reference type; text that starts with `asb:` is always the full form). The resolver reads only the cached dashboard (never a forced scan), the registered sources and their current scope tags, the local machine identity, and the members of `machineGroup.status()`. It reads no app store, opens no session, sends nothing, and writes no state. With `configDir`, it can also read that private live session registry to find a send name. The result (`apiVersion: 1`) is `{ ok: true, form, identity, display, location, availability }` (`referenceUnavailable` too when a short form finds a row with no full reference) or `{ ok: false, code, message }` with HTTP 200. `location` is `local`, `member`, `native-remote-observation` (a remote-cache row), or null when no row was found. `availability` is `found`, `not-found`, `source-disabled`, or `machine-offline`. Full form rules: parse errors give `invalid-reference` or `unsupported-reference-version`; a different `sourceId` gives `scope-mismatch`; no machine identity gives `no-machine-identity`. For this machine, an unregistered source gives `stale-source`, a tag that is not the current tag of the source gives `stale-source-scope`, a disabled source gives `source-disabled` with no row lookup, and else the exact `reference` of the local rows gives `found`, `not-found`, or `ambiguous-identity`. For a member, rows with `referenceUnavailable: 'old-peer'` give `evidence-incomplete`; else its rows give `found` (or `machine-offline` when the member is offline), `not-found`, or `ambiguous-identity`. Another machine gives `unknown-machine`. Short form rules: only local rows with the exact `sessionReference` (and the `--source` source) count; none gives `not-found` with null fields, rows of more than one source give `scope-required`, and more rows of one source give `ambiguous-identity`. The result never has a title, folder, path, cwd, PID, start stamp, socket, key, token, or message text. Limits: the cached dashboard only, so a session that is not in the last scan is `not-found`; the short form works on this machine only; a send name does not prove that a message will arrive.

The CLI takes `configDir` from an absolute `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else `<home>/.claude`. It sends the real path, or the absolute path when the real path lookup fails. The engine reads no registry when `configDir` is absent. With it, the engine adds `sendName` only for an `ok: true`, `availability: 'found'` result at `local` or `member`. It uses `privateRegistry(configDir)`, so a missing or non-private registry gives no name. Exactly one board row must have the resolved full `reference`, also after a short-form lookup. Local rows use `sessionMessageKeys(dashboard).keysByRow`; member rows use the kept member keys. Exactly one live registry record must have a `sessionId` in those keys. A local row excludes `asb-relay` records; a member row accepts only `asb-relay` records. The record must have a non-empty string `name`, and no other live record in that registry can have the same name. Otherwise the result has no `sendName` key. The CLI keeps the name in JSON only when it is a non-empty string of at most 256 printable characters. Its text output adds `Send name: <name>`, else `Send name: none (the session is not open here, or its name is not unique)`. This name is for the caller's native `ListAgents` and `SendMessage`, not an exact ASB Bridge send binding.

`POST /api/agent/resolve-bound` is an internal action route for ASB Bridge, not an `asb agent` command. It has the same Host, Origin, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, token, 8 KiB, and `no-store` rules as `resolve-session`. The body has exactly `reference` (a string), `caller` (exactly `runtimeSessionId`, a UUID, and `configDir`, an absolute path of at most 4096 characters), `msgId` (a UUID), and `remainingMs` (an integer from 0 to 20000), and it can also have `pipeProof` and `hostCheck`, each of which must be exactly `true` (ASB Bridge on Windows sends `pipeProof`; ASB Bridge sends `hostCheck`); another body gives 400 `Invalid resolve request.`, and a thrown error gives 500 `Cannot resolve the session reference.`. `bindLocalTarget` in `src/session-targets.mjs` checks in this order, and the first failure is the reply `{ apiVersion: 1, ok: false, code, message }` (HTTP 200): text that does not start with `asb:` gives `invalid-reference`; then `resolveSessionReference` with the same context as `resolve-session` (its failures pass through, and `evidence-incomplete` becomes `peer-upgrade-required`); a reference of a member gives `member-target`, which tells the bridge to use `send-member` (an older bridge shows it as `... Nothing sent (member-target).`); a platform that is not Linux or Windows gives `unsupported-platform`, and Windows without `pipeProof: true` gives `bridge-upgrade-required`; a type that is not `codex`, `claude-cli`, or `claude-local` gives `unsupported-type`; a `claude-local` reference without `hostCheck: true` gives `host-check-required` (an older ASB Bridge then gets no binding with a key its strict schema would refuse); `source-disabled` and `not-found` pass as codes. Then the caller registry (the real path of `<configDir>/sessions`, a directory of this user with no group or other mode bits; on Windows only a directory; there a record needs a live PID and a `procStart` of 1 to 20 digits that the engine keeps as recorded, because Node cannot read a start time, and it keeps `messagingSocketPath` only for a `cc-msg` pipe path) must hold exactly one live record with the caller ID, and it must be `codex-asb-bridge` or `opencode-asb-bridge`; else `caller-unregistered`. For `codex`, another local codex row with the same app session ID gives `ambiguous-source`. For `claude-cli`, the real registry root of the source (`dirname(projectsDir)/sessions`) must be the caller root, else `native-scope-unavailable` (this also refuses a profile linked with `link-claude`), and another Claude source (enabled or disabled) with the same root gives `ambiguous-source`. For `claude-local`, another local `claude-desktop-code` row with the same app session ID and another reference gives `ambiguous-source`, and the real registry root of the source must be the root of one of the given registries, else `native-scope-unavailable` (there is no other-source rule: two Claude sources can share one root). The candidates are the live records of the caller root that are not `asb-relay` rows, have the app session ID (`sessionId` for `codex` and `claude-cli`, `hostSessionId` for `claude-local`), and have a `messagingSocketPath` (only a `peerProtocol: 1` record has one): none gives `not-live` and more than one gives `ambiguous-identity`. A `codex` target must be `codex-asb-bridge`, and the `receiver` row of `<dataDir>/plugin-state/asb-bridge/<app-id>.sqlite` must have its `pid` and `proc_start`; a `claude-cli` target must be `cli`; a `claude-local` target must have the entrypoint `claude-desktop` or `claude-desktop-3p`, and its `sessionId` must be the `cliSessionId` or one of the `priorCliSessionIds` of the one local row whose `reference` is this reference; else `not-mapped`. The engine keeps these CLI IDs on the row in memory only, under `CLI_SESSION_IDS`; they are not in `/api/dashboard`, the phone API, or the member lists. Success is `{ apiVersion: 1, ok: true, pid, binding: { registryHome, runtimeSessionId, pid, procStart, messagingSocketPath, msgId, expiresAtMs } }`, with the engine pid and `expiresAtMs = now + min(remainingMs, 10000)`; a `claude-local` binding also has `hostSessionId` (the app session ID), and `runtimeSessionId` is the `sessionId` of the target record. The route reads the cached dashboard, the registry records, and the receiver row; it reads no key file, opens no socket, and writes no state. The phone gateway and the member routes never forward it.

`POST /api/agent/list-references` is the internal action route of the references in `list_sessions` (option `listReferences`; 404 when absent). It has the Host, Origin, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, token, 8 KiB, and `no-store` rules of `resolve-bound`. The body is exactly `{ caller }` (`validCaller`), with optional `pipeProof: true` from ASB Bridge on Windows and `hostCheck: true` from ASB Bridge; another body gives 400 `Invalid resolve request.`, and a thrown error gives 500 `Cannot resolve the session reference.`. `referencesForList` in `src/session-targets.mjs` gives `unsupported-platform` off Linux and Windows and `caller-unregistered` without the one caller registration of `resolve-bound` (HTTP 200, `{ apiVersion: 1, ok: false, code, message }`). On Windows, the caller registry has the Windows rules of `resolve-bound`, each local item without `pipeProof: true` gives `bridge-upgrade-required`, and each remote item gives `unsupported-platform`. It reads the caller registry one time for the whole list. Local items: the live records of that registry that are not `asb-relay` rows, have a `messagingSocketPath`, and are not the caller, one item per session ID (at most 256). Two records with one ID give `ambiguous-identity`. An entrypoint that is not `cli`, `codex-asb-bridge`, `claude-desktop`, or `claude-desktop-3p` gives `unsupported-type`, and a Desktop entrypoint with no `hostSessionId` also gives `unsupported-type`. Else the local rows with that `externalId` (`hostSessionId` for a Desktop record): none gives `not-found`, two or more give `ambiguous-source`, one row with no reference gives its `referenceUnavailable` (else `unknown-source`), and one row with a reference goes through `bindLocalTarget`. Remote items: each entry of the relay's `remotePeers()` (at most 256) with the member rows of that machine and `externalId`, with the same rules, checked with `memberTarget`. A check that passes gives the reference, and a refusal gives its code. The reply is `{ apiVersion: 1, ok: true, pid, local: [{ sessionId, reference | referenceUnavailable }], remote: [{ machineId, sessionId, reference | referenceUnavailable }] }`: never a binding, a `returnTo`, a path, a PID, a socket, or a name. The route reads the cached dashboard, the sources and tags, the caller registry, the Claude registry roots, the bridge `receiver` rows, and the relay lists in memory; it reads no key file, opens no socket, and writes no state. The phone gateway and the member routes never forward it.

`POST /api/agent/send-member` is the internal action route of an exact member send (option `sendMember`). It has the same Host, Origin, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, token, and `no-store` rules as `resolve-bound`, with a body of at most 2 MiB (413). The body has exactly `reference` (a string), `caller` (the `resolve-bound` rules, `validCaller`), `mode` (`prompting`, `bypass`, or `unknown`), `message` (checked later by `sendExact`), and `remainingMs` (an integer from 0 to 20000); another body gives 400 `Invalid send request.`, and a thrown error gives 500 `Cannot send the message.`. The route exists whenever the engine runs; without a group or a relay supervisor it answers not sent with `unknown-machine`, never 404. `memberTarget` in `src/session-targets.mjs` checks in this order: text that does not start with `asb:` gives `invalid-reference`; the `resolveSessionReference` failures pass through (`evidence-incomplete` gives `peer-upgrade-required` with no request); this machine's ID gives `local-target`; a platform that is not Linux or Windows gives `unsupported-platform`; a type that is not `codex`, `claude-cli`, or `claude-local` gives `unsupported-type`; the caller registry check of `resolve-bound` gives `caller-unregistered`. It does not use the cached member rows: the member decides `not-found`, and the engine decides `machine-offline` from the poller. The sender name is the name of the caller record (control characters become spaces, at most 128 characters, else `OpenCode` when the caller is `opencode-asb-bridge`, else `Codex`). Then `sendExact` of the relay supervisor gets the budget minus the time that the engine used. The reply (HTTP 200) is `{ apiVersion: 1, pid, msgId, outcome }` with `outcome` `submitted`, `unknown`, or `not-sent`, and `code` and `message` only for `not-sent` (`notSentMessage`: the bound codes first, then the resolver codes). The phone gateway and the member routes never forward it.

Both routes below require a nonempty configured token and the exact `X-ASB-Source-Token`; they return `Cache-Control: no-store`. Status uses the read-origin checks. UI reports use the action Host/Origin checks and the normal body limit.

| Loopback route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/agent/status` | `apiVersion: 1`, `pid`, `mode` (`service` or `desktop`), `appVersion`, `rootDir`, `sourcesPath`, saved `sourcesRevision`, actual cached dashboard `backendRevision` (or `null`), and `ui.clients`. No token is returned. |
| `POST /api/agent/ui` | Exact body `{clientId,sourcesRevision,profilesRevision,profilesVisible,dirty}`, or exactly `{clientId,closed:true}` from a window that closes. `clientId` is a UUID; revisions are `null` or 64 lowercase hex characters; flags are strict booleans. Replies `{received:true,reportedAtMs}`. The server keeps at most 16 recent clients and expires reports after 45 seconds. While an event stream `/api/events?ui=<clientId>` with the client ID is open, the client does not expire, and `/api/agent/status` shows its last report with the current time as `reportedAtMs`; when the last such stream closes, the 45 seconds count from that moment. The `connected` event of such a stream has `uiClient: true`. The closed form removes its client at once; for 45 seconds (at most 16 closed clients) a later report of that client is ignored, because a report can still be on its way. |

Native clients report received dashboard and Profiles revisions, visibility, and dirty state on changes, and send one last report when their window closes. The board names its client in its event stream (`EventStream` `ui_client`), and passes `source_changed` as its `on_connected` callback so a new connection also reads the list; while the engine of the stream holds the client (`holds_ui`), the board reports once after each new stream connection and then only on changes. With no stream, or with an older engine, it repeats the report about every 25 seconds through its timer. With a connected stream, a failed report goes again at the next timer tick. Reports include no unsaved field contents. CLI write verification checks saved settings and the cached backend separately, then reports UI as `verified`, `pending`, `dirty`, or `not-open`. UI verification requires a recent report with the target dashboard revision and, if Profiles is visible, its target table revision. After a write, the report must also be at least as new as the write request. These are acknowledgments of received state, not proof of visual display. UI status is reported separately from command success, which requires saved and backend verification.

### Loopback phone and service routes

These routes exist only when the server has a phone gateway (the engine). `npm start` has no gateway, so they give 404. All of them need the header `X-ASB-Source-Token` when a token is set, also the GET routes; else the reply is 403. GET routes use the read checks above. POST routes use the action Host and Origin checks and strict bodies (exact keys, else 400). Replies have `Cache-Control: no-store`.

| Loopback route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/phone` | Gateway `status()`: `enabled`, `bindAddresses`, `port`, `addresses` (`address`, `interface`, `kind`), `listening` (`kind`, `address`), `running`, `error`, `warning`, `fingerprint`, `devices` (ID, name, times), and `pairing.expiresAtMs`. It never has a token, token hash, or pairing code. |
| `POST /api/phone/settings` | `{"enabled":bool,"bindAddresses":[str],"port":int}`. `bindAddresses` has 0 to 8 different addresses, and each must be in `addresses` now or already saved. When `enabled` is true, it must have at least one address. The port must be 1024 to 65535 and not the ASB port. Saves, restarts the listener, and replies with `status()`. |
| `POST /api/phone/pairing` | `{}`. Needs a running listener. Replies `{ uri, expiresAtMs }`. |
| `POST /api/phone/devices/<id>/revoke` | `{}`. `<id>` is 12 lowercase hex. Removes that phone and replies with `status()`. |
| `GET /api/service` | `{ service, pid }`. `service` is true only in the engine. `findRunningService()` uses it. |
| `POST /api/engine/stop` | Engine only. Needs a nonempty token, the action Host and Origin checks, and the exact body `{}` (else 400). Replies `{"stopping":true}`, then writes `stopped.json` before shutdown and exits with code 0. The marker has schema `1` and the SHA-256 hex of its source token. A temporary file and a rename publish it with mode `0600`. A write error is logged once and shutdown continues. SIGTERM and SIGINT write no marker. The phone gateway never forwards it. |

### Palette route

The native window shares its resolved palette, so the phone can use the same colors. This route exists also when the server has no phone gateway.

| Loopback route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `POST /api/palette` | Body exactly `{"mode","colors"}`. `mode` is `gnome` or `custom`. `colors` has exactly `background`, `text`, `accent`, `muted`, `divider`, `success`, and `warning`, each `#RRGGBB`. Else 400. Uses the action Host and Origin checks, and needs the header `X-ASB-Source-Token` when a token is set (else 403). The server saves the colors in lowercase and replies `{ changed }`. A change sends the normal `dashboard` event. |

`GET /api/dashboard` has the top-level field `palette`: the last saved `{ mode, colors }`, or `null` before the first publish. The field is part of the `ETag` content, so a palette change gives a new tag. It also makes the saved list again, because the server compares the palette object. In GNOME mode the window sends the `native_colors()` values with the GNOME `success_color` and `warning_color`. In Custom mode it sends the saved custom colors, with `success` and `warning` equal to `accent`. The window sends at start, after Apply theme, after Reset to GNOME, and after a GNOME dark style or accent change. It sends only a palette that is different from the last good send. A failed send writes one line to stderr, and the next apply tries again.

### Phone gateway

`createPhoneGateway` in `src/phone-gateway.mjs` makes the phone listeners, separate from the loopback server. It is off by default. `listPhoneAddresses()` lists each usable IPv4 address as `{ address, interface, kind }`, sorted by kind (`lan`, `zerotier`, `tailscale`), then by interface name:

| Kind | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `tailscale` | The interface name starts with `tailscale` and the address is in `100.64.0.0/10`. |
| `zerotier` | The interface name starts with `zt` and the address is RFC 1918 private (`10/8`, `172.16/12`, `192.168/16`). |
| `lan` | Any other interface with an RFC 1918 private address. |

A `100.64.0.0/10` address on an interface that is not Tailscale is never listed, because carrier NAT and mobile hotspots also use this range. Loopback and public addresses are never listed. The gateway never binds `0.0.0.0`.

When `enabled` is true, the gateway listens on each address in `bindAddresses` that is listed now, all on the same port (default `4630`), with the same certificate and the same handler. It skips a selected address that is not listed now, and `status().error` names it and says that ASB listens on it when it appears; the other addresses still listen. A port in use also goes in `status().error`. `running` is true when at least one listener listens. `status().listening` lists the listeners that listen now.

Every 30 seconds (an `unref` timer from `start()` to `stop()`), the gateway compares the selected addresses that are listed now with the set that the last restart used. Only a changed set restarts the listeners, so an address that comes up after the start (for example ZeroTier after `asb.service`) gets a listener, and an address that goes away loses its listener. A restart closes the open phone connections and ends a pairing code. A busy port or a missing `openssl` keeps the same set, so the check does not retry it until the addresses change or the user applies the settings. Preferences > Phone reads the status and the address list again each time the page opens. A form with no unsaved edits follows the server; a form with unsaved edits keeps its switch, port, and checks, and a new address shows unchecked. After Apply the form shows the saved values. A saved address that is not listed now stays as a checked row named "Not on this PC now", and `POST /api/phone/settings` accepts it.

`phone.json` is schema 2 with `bindAddresses`. A schema 1 file (`bindAddress`) loads as schema 2 (an empty `bindAddress` gives `[]`), and the next save writes schema 2.

TLS uses `node:https` with `minVersion: 'TLSv1.3'` on every listener. Each listener asks for a client certificate (`requestCert: true, rejectUnauthorized: false`): phones send none, and only the group member routes check it. On first start, `openssl req` (run with `execFile`, no shell, 20-second timeout) creates a self-signed P-256 certificate for ten years. The pin is the SHA-256 of the full certificate, as 64 lowercase hex characters. When `openssl` fails, phone access stays off with `ASB cannot create the phone certificate. Install openssl.` The request timeout is 15 seconds, the header timeout is 10 seconds, and the keep-alive timeout is 65 seconds. Every reply has `cache-control: no-store` and `x-content-type-options: nosniff`.

The gateway handles each request in this order:

1. It closes the socket when the remote address is not allowed on that listener (also as `::ffff:a.b.c.d`). A `lan` or `zerotier` listener allows loopback and RFC 1918 callers. A `tailscale` listener allows `100.64.0.0/10` and RFC 1918 callers. No listener allows public callers.
2. Throttle: 20 auth failures (401) or pair failures from one address in 60 seconds give `429` for 60 seconds. The map keeps at most 256 addresses.
3. A bad URL gives 400. A path outside the allowlist gives 404. A wrong method gives 405.
4. Each route except pairing needs `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, with a 43-character base64url token. The gateway compares its SHA-256 with each saved hash in constant time. No match gives 401.
5. POST bodies are limited to 64 KiB (413).

| Gateway route | Action |
| --- | --- |
| `POST /phone/v1/pair` | Body exactly `{"code","deviceName"}`. Gives the phone a new token. |
| `GET /phone/v1/hello` | `{ api: 1, asbVersion, deviceId, serverName, addresses, port, machineId, engines }`. `addresses` is `[{ kind, address }]` for the listeners that listen now. `machineId` is the first 16 characters of the gateway fingerprint. `engines` is `[]` with no group, else the active members `[{ machineId, name, port, fingerprint, addresses }]` (the member `certFingerprint`, and the poller's last addresses); the entry of this engine has the live listeners and port. A phone token that another member shares gets the same reply. The app updates its saved address list from the paired PC's reply at each app start, at each start of the session poll, in Settings, and after the first good dashboard after a network change. |
| `GET /phone/v1/dashboard` | Proxy to `GET /api/dashboard`. Only the query `force=1` passes. |
| `POST /phone/v1/threads/<id>/<action>` | Proxy to the same loopback thread route. `<action>` is `open`, `mark-read`, `mark-unread`, `pin`, `unpin`, `move-pin`, `discard-result`, `keep-result`, `drawer-in`, or `drawer-out`. |
| `POST /phone/v1/settings/unread` | Proxy to `POST /api/settings/unread`. |
| `GET /phone/v1/activity/messages` | Proxy to `GET /api/activity/messages`. Only a valid `period` (`24h`, `7d`, `30d`; default `7d`) passes; another value gives 400 and every other query value is dropped. The gateway asks for `/api/activity/messages?period=<period>&text=0`, so the reply has no `text`, `fromLabel`, or `toLabel`, and the engine reads no text for it. Gzip as the dashboard. |
| `GET /phone/v1/activity/usage` | Proxy to `GET /api/activity/usage`. Only a valid `period` (`24h`, `7d`, `30d`, `90d`, `all`; default `30d`) passes, as above. Gzip as the dashboard. |

The proxy calls `127.0.0.1:<ASB port>` and sets `Host` itself. POST also gets the local `Origin` and `content-type: application/json`. Only `If-None-Match` passes from the phone; cookies, `Authorization`, and other headers do not. Only `content-type` and `etag` come back from the loopback reply, plus `content-encoding: gzip` for a gzip phone and a 200 list or activity reply. Every dashboard reply that the loopback server answers has `Vary: Accept-Encoding`. The gateway's own error replies (401, 405, 429, 502) do not. When the phone accepts gzip in `Accept-Encoding` (`gzip` with a q-value above 0, or `*` with a q-value above 0 when `gzip` is not listed) and the loopback status is 200, the gateway asks the engine for gzip and passes its gzip bytes through unchanged, with `Content-Encoding: gzip`. If an older engine sends plain JSON, the gateway streams it through gzip as before. For a phone that does not accept gzip, the gateway does not ask the engine for gzip and sends plain JSON. The `ETag` stays the same. A 304 reply has no body and no `Content-Encoding`. Other routes are not compressed. The Android app sets no `Accept-Encoding` header, so OkHttp sends `gzip` and decompresses the reply. A loopback error after a gzip reply starts closes the connection. A failed loopback request gives 502, and the proxy timeout is 30 seconds. The loopback server applies its normal checks. The gateway cannot reach `/api/agent/*`, `/api/sources`, `/api/phone*`, `/api/group*`, `/api/engine/*`, `/api/service`, `/api/palette`, or `/api/events`.

Android and the machine group (plan 15.2): the app keeps the pairing as before and saves the other engines of the last hello (`otherEngines`: valid IDs, pins, ports, names, and at least one address; not the paired pin; at most 32) and the engine that answered last. A GET tries the current engine, then the paired PC, then the others in list order, each at its own addresses, port, and pinned certificate. When the current engine is another one, the paired PC goes first again once a minute (per AsbApi object), so the phone returns to it when it is back. The first engine that answers becomes current. A 401 from another engine is not an answer (the group did not share the phone yet, or that engine left); a 401 from the paired PC revokes as before. An authenticated POST (a row action or a setting) goes only to the engine of the dashboard on screen, at its first address, because remote row IDs differ per engine. The pair request has no token and goes to the paired PC: it tries every address of the link in order, and the first HTTP reply ends the loop. The PC accepts a code one time only (a used code gets 403 and adds no device), so a second address cannot add a second phone. An `ETag` goes only to the engine that gave it. Only the paired PC's hello changes the saved addresses; any hello changes the engine list. Each engine has its own read marks, so the notification baseline belongs to one engine (`baselineEngine`): the first dashboard of another engine starts a new baseline and posts nothing. The baseline and the notification tags use the row key (`globalId`, else `id`), so a change of engine does not repeat a notification. The same read of the live service or the check worker (same baseline, same conditions as the post) also cancels the active notifications (tag `<key>:<kind>`) of each row whose bit for that kind is clear in the new baseline value that `update` saves (`cleared` in `notify/Events.kt`, `Notifier.cancelCleared`, one summary update, no notification permission needed). The new bit decides, not a change from the old baseline: the open session screen saves a cleared bit first and cancels nothing, so its notifications go at the first read after the app is in the background (1 to 30 seconds with the live service, else at the 15-minute check). A usage limit does not hide a mark: a limit stop sets the Failed bit once, and the reset changes no bit; an archived row has no bits. A row missing from the read and a first read with no baseline cancel nothing. A remote row cancels only on a read from the paired PC (`engine` is empty): the paired PC marks its remote rows with its own tracker, and the phone posts from those marks. Another engine starts a remote row that it sees for the first time with no mark, so its clear bits prove nothing and a remote row cancels nothing on its read. A local row cancels on every engine. Android keeps about 50 notifications for one app, so a notification that stays can make Android drop new ones. Remote and CLI cards are view only (option D, the rules of `is_view_only`, `row_where`, and `view_only_tone`): the machine glyph and name, or the terminal glyph and `CLI`, before the age; ` · <name>` after the state in Compact; the quiet colors with no attention; no Discard on a remote row; the inspector button `On <name>` or `Runs in a terminal`, disabled. Settings > Connection lists the machines with Online, Offline, or Checking (a hello to each engine at once) and marks the one In use.

Pairing: `createPairing` makes one code of 20 random bytes (base64url). It is valid for 10 minutes and for one use. A new code replaces the old one, and 8 wrong codes void it. The QR link is `asb://pair?v=2&a=<list>&p=<port>&f=<fingerprint>&c=<code>&n=<host name>`. `<list>` is the listening addresses as comma-separated `kind:address` items in the kind order above (for example `lan:192.168.50.4,zerotier:10.147.17.4,tailscale:100.101.102.103`), URL-encoded as one value. Version 1 links had `h=<one address>`. The Android app reads this link from its own scanner (Scan QR code: CameraX preview and ZXing decode on the phone, CAMERA permission asked on first use), from the phone camera or any QR scanner app (a VIEW intent filter for `asb://pair`), or from a pasted link. All of them only fill the Check the PC screen; the user compares the fingerprint and pairs. The scanner-open state is in `PairingViewModel`, so a rotation keeps it open, and a result after Back or unpair is ignored. A good pair request saves a new device with a 256-bit random token and replies with the token once. The PC saves only the SHA-256 of the token. The device limit is eight (409). The device name is trimmed, has no control characters, and has at most 64 characters. `lastSeenAtMs` is saved at most once per 10 minutes per phone, and on stop. Revoke removes the device, so its token gives 401 at once.

### Machine group

The engine can join other ASB engines in one machine group. Each engine then shows the sessions of the other members as remote cards. The engine makes one `createMachineGroup` object in `src/machine-group.mjs` before the gateway. The object reads the gateway and the server lazily. It owns `group.json`, the invite, the join and leave flows, the member pollers, and the gateway member callbacks.

The machine identity is the phone certificate. `machineId` is the first 16 hex characters of its SHA-256 fingerprint. `group.json` is next to `phone.json`, mode `0600`, with an atomic write. An invalid file moves to `group.json.bad` with a warning. The file has `schema: 1`, `groupId` (16 hex), `self` (`machineId`, `name`), `members` (with this machine: `machineId`, `name`, `certFingerprint`, `addresses`, `port`, `addedBy`, `addedAtMs`), `removed` (tombstones by `machineId` and `certFingerprint`), `phones` (`deviceId`, `name`, `tokenHash`, `addedBy`, `addedAtMs`), and `removedPhones`. Loads and received groups are validated strictly: exact keys, hex lengths, address kinds with the gateway address rules, ports, names, and no control characters. A received group that fails is ignored.

Merge rules: a union of each list. When two entries have one key, the entry with the smaller `addedAtMs`, then the smaller `addedBy`, stays. A tombstone always wins and stays; a tombstoned entry leaves the list. Limits are 32 members, 64 phones, and 256 entries in each tombstone list (the oldest go first). A group with another `groupId` changes nothing. Names match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,31}$` and are unique among active members (case-insensitive). A merged group that fails the check of `group.json` (for example two members whose names differ only in letter case) is not saved or applied: the engine keeps its group and logs `ASB did not apply a group update from another machine: the merged group is not valid.` `suggestName` removes other characters and adds `-2`, `-3`, and so on.

Flows:

- **Invite** (`POST /api/group/invite`): needs network access on (the gateway listens), else 409 `Turn on network access first: asb network enable <address>.` The first invite makes the group with this machine and its paired phones. Each later invite saves the current addresses and port of this machine in its own member entry when they changed (the entry keeps its `addedAtMs` and `addedBy`), so a machine that joins later polls the new ones; a list that the group cannot hold gives 409 `ASB cannot save these network addresses in the group.` The invite code is 20 random bytes (base64url), valid 10 minutes, one use, and void after 8 wrong codes; a new invite replaces it. The reply is `{ link, fingerprint, expiresAtMs }`. The link is `asb://join?v=1&g=<groupId>&a=<kind:address,...>&p=<port>&f=<fingerprint>&c=<code>&n=<inviter name>`.
- **Join** (`POST /api/group/join` `{ link, name }`): the engine parses the link and refuses when network access is off or when it is in a group (`Leave the current group first.`). An empty name gives the host name suggestion. It posts `/peer/v1/join` to the inviter, pinned to `f`, with its own certificate. On 200 it validates the group, saves it with `self`, adds its paired phones, and starts the pollers. A 409 passes `{ error, suggestion }` through. No answer gives 502 `The other machine did not answer.`
- **Inviter side of join:** the throttle, then the code (wrong code: 401). A removed certificate gives 409 `This machine was removed. Reset its identity and join again.` An active member (a joiner whose first reply was lost) gets the same `200 { groupId, group }` as a new join, with no change to the saved group, and the invite is used; the joiner takes its saved name from the reply and completes its join. A taken or invalid name gives 409 with `suggestion`. A full group gives 409. Then the member is added with `machineId` from the caller certificate, never from the body.
- **Remove** (`POST /api/group/remove` `{ machineId }`): adds a tombstone and stops that poller. This machine cannot remove itself (`Use asb group leave to leave the group.`).
- **Leave** (`POST /api/group/leave` `{}`): posts `/peer/v1/leave` to one member that answers in 4 s (best effort), deletes `group.json`, and stops the pollers. The certificate stays, so paired phones keep working. A new join after a removal needs a new identity: if `asb group status` still shows a group, run `asb group leave`; then stop ASB (`asb engine stop`), run `asb group reset-identity`, and start ASB again.
- **Tombstone for this machine:** when a merged group has a tombstone for this machine (`machineId` and certificate), or when a member answers 410, the engine deletes `group.json`, stops the pollers, and logs `ASB: this machine was removed from the machine group.`
- **Phones:** a phone paired while in the group gets a `phones` entry; a revoke adds a `removedPhones` tombstone. The gateway also accepts the token of an active group phone (by `tokenHash`), with no last-seen save.

Member routes on the gateway listeners (mutual TLS). The caller fingerprint is the SHA-256 of the client certificate DER. Every route except `join` needs the certificate of an active member. A removed certificate gets 410 `This machine was removed from the group.`, which does not count toward the throttle; the poller then stops. Other callers get 401, which counts toward the throttle. Bodies are limited to 64 KiB (2 MiB for `deliver`) with exact keys. Replies have `no-store` and `nosniff`.

| Gateway route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `POST /peer/v1/join` | Any client certificate (none gives 401). Body `{ code, name, addresses, port }`. `200 { groupId, group }` (the group without `self`), 401, 409 `{ error, suggestion? }`, or 429. |
| `GET /peer/v1/hello` | `{ api: 1, machineId, name, groupId, asbVersion, addresses, port }`. A removed certificate gets 410 on this route and on `group`, `sessions`, and `leave`. The optional query `?a=<kind:address,...>&p=<port>` (the join-link form, URL-encoded) has the caller's current network: before the reply, `peer.seen` saves it in the caller's own active entry (only `addresses` and `port`, with the group checks; `addedAtMs` and `addedBy` stay). An invalid query, an unknown or removed caller, or the same values save nothing, and the reply is the same. |
| `GET /peer/v1/group` | The group without `self`. |
| `GET /peer/v1/sessions` | The session export with an `ETag`; `If-None-Match` gives 304. Gzip when the caller accepts it, with `Vary: Accept-Encoding`. |
| `POST /peer/v1/leave` | `{}`. Adds a tombstone for the caller. `{ left: true }`. |
| `GET /peer/v1/peers` | The relay's `GET /peers`: `{ peers: [{ sessionId, name, agent, status, remoteControl }] }`. No relay: `{ peers: [] }`. |
| `GET /peer/v1/bridge` | Only when the engine gives `peer.bridge`. The ASB Bridge report of this machine (`report()` of `src/bridge-coverage.mjs`): `{ api: 1, asbVersion, checkedAtMs, expectedVersion, apps }`, at most 32 apps of `{ sourceId, label, provider, state, version, live, command }`, with no path and no install data. It waits at most 2.5 s for a check; with no result yet it gives 503 `ASB is checking ASB Bridge.`, and the asking machine shows `checking`. A failed check gives `apps: []` and `error: 'check'`. A member with an older ASB gives 404, and the asking machine shows `old`. |
| `GET /peer/v1/activity?period=24h\|7d\|30d\|90d\|all` | Only when the engine gives `peer.activity`. Default `7d`; another period gives 400. The local records of this member only, gzip when the caller accepts it: `{ messages, usage, scan }`. `messages` are the message records of the last min(period, 30 days) (none for `all`) with `id`, `from`, `to`, `atMs`, `status`, `reason`, `direction` (`in`, `out`, or `both`), `hash` (the text hash), `label` (the name from the message, or `''`), and `text` (the body for the newest 200 records, at most 2,000 characters; else `null`), at most 20,000. `usage` is the hourly token usage per app and model (`hourMs` is the UTC hour start, four token counts, no cost; per key the larger of the transcripts and the [usage ledger](#usage-ledger)), at most the newest 50,000 records. `scan` is the progress of its usage scan. No path, title, or cost, and no text of older messages. |
| `POST /peer/v1/deliver` | The relay body (plan 9.7). `sender.machineId` must be the caller's machine ID, else 403 `The sender machine does not match.` Then the relay's `POST /deliver`. `200 { ok: true, outcome: 'socket-written' }` or `200 { ok: false, reason }`; no relay or a relay error gives `target-unavailable`. An invalid body gives 400 `Invalid relay request.` |
| `POST /peer/v2/resolve-session` | Read-only, body limit 64 KiB. Body `{ api: 2, target: { sourceId, scopeTag, type, appId } }` (four strings; the member checks them with `formatReference` and its own machine ID). `peerResolveExact` binds the reference with `bindMemberTarget` (a new `msgId`, `remainingMs: 0`) and drops the binding. `200 { api: 2, outcome: 'ready' }`, or `200 { api: 2, outcome: 'unavailable', reason }`: a binder refusal gives its code, a throw gives `target-unavailable`, and a relay that does not run gives `relay-unavailable`. An invalid body gives 400 `Invalid relay request.` |
| `POST /peer/v2/deliver` | Body limit 2 MiB. Body `{ api: 2, target, sender, message, remainingMs }`: `sender` and `message` have the v1 rules, and `remainingMs` is an integer from 0 to 10000. `sender.machineId` must be the caller's machine ID, else 403. `peerDeliverExact` sets the deadline `now + min(remainingMs - 500, 8000)`, binds the reference with the message ID, and limits `expiresAtMs` to `deadline - 5500`; a time that has already passed gives `binding-expired` with no relay call. Then the relay's `POST /deliver-bound` until the deadline. `200 { api: 2, msgId, outcome: 'submitted' | 'unknown' }` or `200 { api: 2, msgId, outcome: 'not-sent', reason }`. A binder throw gives `target-unavailable`, no relay gives `relay-unavailable`, and a relay HTTP 400, 401, or 404 (an older `relay.js`) gives `relay-unavailable`. Another relay error or reply gives `unknown`. A body with exactly `api: 2` and `control` is the receipt of an exact send of this engine (`peerReceipt`, see "Exact member sends"): `control` has the v1 control rules (`action: 'peer_message_status'`, `orig_msg_id`, `status`, and the optional `status_detail`, `reason`, `drop_reason`, `dropped_msg_ids`) and no other key, so it cannot carry text or name a destination. Another body gives 400. The reply is `200 { api: 2, msgId: <orig_msg_id>, outcome: 'submitted' \| 'unknown' }` or `200 { api: 2, msgId, outcome: 'not-sent', reason: 'binding-expired' \| 'target-unavailable' }`. |

The two v2 routes exist only when the engine gives `peer.resolveExact` and `peer.deliverExact`, with the member checks of the v1 routes (405, 410, 401, 413). Every other `/peer/v2/*` path gives 404, and the v2 handlers never answer 404, so 404 always means "this member has no v2". `reason` is one bounded enum for both replies (`EXACT_REASONS` in `src/machine-relay.mjs`); a binder code outside it gives `target-unavailable`.

The asking side (`activity(period)` of `createMachineGroup`) asks each active member in parallel with a 5 s timeout and a 16 MiB reply limit (`MAX_ACTIVITY_BYTES`). It keeps each reply for 30 s per member and period. `validActivity` keeps only valid records with only the contract fields (at most 20,000 messages and 50,000 usage records). It keeps `hash` only when it is 16 lowercase hex digits, `label` only up to 120 characters (else `''`), and `text` only up to 2,001 characters (else `null`). A usage record can have any app name of at most 200 characters: the windows and the phone show an app that they do not know as Other. A member that does not answer is `offline` and keeps its last reply and the time of that reply (`lastReportAtMs`). The engine merges the messages by message ID (a receiver row wins the status) and prices the member usage with its own rates. So the Messages window of one machine shows the text of messages between sessions of other machines. The member routes have no thread route; the thread of the PC reads the merged records and the member texts.

The peer list `agent` field is `codex` or `claude`. `agentOf` in `bridge/src/relay.ts` gives `codex` for `entrypoint` `codex-asb-bridge` and `claude` for every other entrypoint, including `opencode-asb-bridge`. So a remote OpenCode session shows as `agent: 'claude'` on the wire, and an OpenCode sender uses `agent: 'claude'`. This is a label only (Q3); a future release can add `opencode` to the enum.

The export (`exportLocalSessions` in `src/machine-peers.mjs`) is computed in the server's `loadDashboard` wrapper from the loaded dashboard, before the merge. It has `api`, `machineId`, `name`, `generatedAtMs`, `refreshIntervalMs`, `rows`, `keys`, and `incoming`. Archived rows, their keys, and their incoming messages are not exported, so the Archive filter of a machine shows only its own archived rows. `refreshIntervalMs` comes from the exported rows only: a member polls this machine every two seconds only while this machine has a Working row. Rows have only these fields: `id`, `externalId`, `provider`, `providerLabel`, `surface`, `title`, `cwd`, `projectName`, `archived`, `updatedAtMs`, `subagentCount`, `state`, `reason`, `workingSinceMs`, `nativeUnread`, `readStatus`, `questionPending`, `actionRequired`, `lastOutcome`, `failedAtMs`, `completionAtMs`, `limitResetsAtMs`, the `source*` label fields, the optional `referenceScope` (32 lowercase hex characters, else `validExport` drops the row), and the optional `referenceUnavailable` (a string). `reference` and `sessionReference` are not exported. Remote and remote-cache rows are not exported. `keys` has the native message keys of each row; `incoming` has the messages whose sender is not a local key. A member drops a reply of more than 5000 rows or 4 MiB (`MAX_REPLY_BYTES`) whole, and then it shows this machine as offline. So `exportBody` sends a longer list as only its newest rows (by `updatedAtMs`): at most 5000 rows, and rows and keys of at most 3.5 MiB, which leaves room for the incoming messages (at most 500) and the envelope. It keeps the order of the rows, and it cuts the `keys` and the `incoming` messages of the rows that it cuts. The `ETag` is the SHA-256 of the JSON without `generatedAtMs`. The `sessions` callback first asks the server for its dashboard, so the export is current.

The peer client (`peerRequest`) uses `https.request` with TLS 1.3, its own certificate, and a 4 s timeout. A POST uses `agent: false`: each POST has a new connection, because a reused socket that the member closed can fail after the hand-off, and a POST must not go out two times. A GET keeps its connection: there is one keep-alive `https.Agent` for each member address, port, pin, and own certificate fingerprint (the agents belong to the process, so several engines in one test process never share a client certificate), with the secure context of that certificate made one time (`minVersion: 'TLSv1.3'` is an option of this context, and `maxCachedSessions: 0` gives a full handshake on each new connection). An agent keeps at most 2 free sockets and closes a socket after 30 s without traffic (`PEER_IDLE_SOCKET_MS`), before the 65 s keep-alive timeout of the gateway. A request with another pin uses its own agent, so a member at the same address and port as another member never closes the kept sockets of that member. `closePeerAgents` closes the sockets of a member when its poller stops (the member left the group, its pin changed, or the engine stopped). On `secureConnect` of a new socket, the client compares the SHA-256 of the server certificate with `certFingerprint` and sends nothing on a mismatch (`pin-mismatch`); a socket that passes keeps the pin as a mark. A kept socket carries a request only when its mark is the pin of the request: an open socket with no mark or with another pin gives `pin-mismatch` and no request byte goes out. A GET that fails on a kept socket before the first byte of the reply (for example `ECONNRESET` after the member closed the socket) goes again one time on a new connection (`agent: false`), in the rest of the same timeout. It tries the last good address, then `lan`, `zerotier`, and `tailscale`. A GET tries each address for the full timeout. A POST has one total timeout for all addresses and goes to the next address only when the request did not go out (a refused connection, a pin mismatch, or a timeout before `request.end`). Before `request.end`, each address gets the time that is left divided by the number of addresses that are left, so one address that hangs does not use all the time. After `request.end`, the request can use all the time that is left. An error after `request.end` has `handedOff: true` and ends the attempt, so a member never gets the same POST two times. Replies are limited to 4 MiB after gunzip. One poller runs for each active member except this machine and keeps the last good address in its state (`lastAddress`, in memory only, in no reply or export): sessions every `max(2000, refreshIntervalMs)` ms with the `ETag`, group every 30 s (merged and saved when it changed), and hello at start and every 60 s.
