The hello request carries the current addresses and port of this machine from the gateway status in its query (no query when network access is off or the group cannot hold them), so a member that cannot reach an old port or address of this machine learns the new one when this machine reaches it. The addresses and port of a hello reply stay in memory; the poller takes the saved ones again only when the saved entry of that member changes. A failed poll waits 5 s, 10 s, then 30 s. A member is offline after 3 failed polls in a row and online after the next good reply. An invalid export counts as a failed poll. All timers are `unref`. A new export or an online change marks the dashboard dirty.

The merge (`mergePeerRows`) runs before `PendingTracker.observe`, so Read, Unread, Pin, the drawer, Mute, sounds, and the phone work on remote rows with the normal rules. They change only this engine's `pending.json`. A remote row has the ID `m:<machineId>:<row id>`, `remote: true`, `machineId` and `machineName` of the member, `globalId`, `canOpen: false`, and `appDeepLink: ""`. It keeps the member's `providerLabel`, `surface`, and `source*` fields. The observer makes the row's `reference` itself from the pinned member's `machineId`, the row's `sourceId` and `referenceScope`, the type, and `externalId`, and then removes `referenceScope` from the row. A member-sent `reference`, machine ID, or machine name is never used. A member with no tag (an older ASB) gives `referenceUnavailable: 'old-peer'`; an invalid app ID gives `invalid-app-id`. A member-sent `referenceUnavailable` only blocks a reference: when the row has no tag and the code is `ambiguous-source` (a shared CLI row of the member), the row gets `ambiguous-source` instead of `old-peer`. In all other cases the observer ignores the member code. The observer also gives a member row its own checked `sessionReference` (the provider host, because member rows have no `sourceName`). The rows of an offline member are Unknown with `<name> is not reachable.` for 10 minutes after its last good poll; then they leave. Such a row has the symbol `OFFLINE_MEMBER`: `PendingTracker.observe` does not reset its Working mark, and the sound watcher gets no update for it. So a task that ends during a short outage gets its mark and one Done sound when the member is back, and a task that ended before it plays no sound again. A local remote-cache row leaves when a member row has one of its `bridge:` keys. Messages use one key map of local and member keys (a key with two rows is not used), with the normal window, limit, and order. Open and Discard refuse a remote row (`Discard does not work on a session on another machine.`, 400). The dashboard also keeps `Map<rowId, keys[]>` for the member rows on the board under the non-enumerable `MEMBER_MESSAGE_KEYS` symbol. The request snapshot keeps these private symbols. This does not change the local export or the message merge.

`GET /api/group` gives `{ groupId, self: { machineId, name }, networkEnabled, members: [{ machineId, name, online, lastOkAtMs, addresses, isSelf, asbVersion }], phones: [{ deviceId, name }], relay? }`. `asbVersion` is the version of this ASB for this machine, and for a member the version of its last valid `hello` (kept in memory only, like the addresses; `""` before it). `relay` is `machineRelay.status()` (`{ state: 'running' | 'stopped' | 'no-bun' | 'not-running', bunFolders? }`); the server adds it only when the group has a member that is not this machine, and the gateway never serves this route. Without a group, `groupId` is `""`, `self.name` is the suggestion, and the lists are empty. The group routes use the phone route checks (source token, local Host, local Origin for POST, `no-store`, exact bodies). Errors are 400, 409 `{ error, suggestion? }`, or 502.

Preferences > Machines (`MachinesEditor` in `scripts/asb-native.py`, the approved option A) is made on its first selection, like Phone. Each open reads `GET /api/group`; while the page shows, one `GLib.timeout_add_seconds` timer reads it again every 5 s, and the timer stops when the page hides or Preferences closes. A poll shows no loading text and keeps the open panel and its form text. **Add a machine** posts `invite` and shows the link, a 156 px QR code (`qr_matrix`, as Phone), and the fingerprint; a poll with a new member closes it with `Joined: <name>`, and the expiry closes it. **Join a group** checks the pasted link with `join_link_summary` (exact keys `v,g,a,p,f,c,n`, `v` 1, 64 hex `f`, a valid `n`; the engine checks the rest) and posts `{ link, name }` only after **It is the same**. Remove and Leave use an `Adw.AlertDialog`, or an `Adw.MessageDialog` on Libadwaita 1.4; the page closes it when it hides. The pure helpers before the GTK import are `machine_status_text`, `machine_addresses_text`, `machine_member_subtitle`, `machine_phones_text`, `machines_view`, `machine_confirm_body`, `join_link_summary`, and `machine_invite_text`. `request_json` treats `/api/group*` like the phone routes: it sends the source token, a POST reply needs no `changed`, an error shows the server `error` (with `Try the name <suggestion>.` for a valid suggestion), and join and leave wait 30 s instead of 15 s. The page also reads `GET /api/bridge` when it opens and at each poll, one request at a time; a reply after the page hides is dropped, and an error or an invalid reply keeps the last data with no message. `machine_bridge_line` adds one line to the subtitle of this machine and of each member: `Bridge: not checked while offline`, `Bridge: unknown, needs ASB 1.12+` (with ` · N live` from the relay count), `Bridge: <error>`, `Bridge: checking…`, `Bridge: no app profiles`, `Bridge: not installed` (every app is missing), or the apps, for example `Bridge: Codex ready · Codex Personal hooks not trusted · OpenCode old version`. A poll with the same lines keeps the rows and their focus. `request_json` sends the source token only for a bridge POST, and POST `/api/bridge/install` waits 420 s.

Preferences > Usage (`UsageBackupEditor` in `scripts/asb-native.py`) is made on its first selection, like Phone and Machines. Each open reads `GET /api/usage/backup`; one request or file job runs at a time, and a reply after the page hides is dropped. The settings list has **Daily backup** (`Adw.SwitchRow`), **Time** (two wrapping `Gtk.SpinButton`s that show two digits), **Files to keep** (`Adw.SpinRow`, 1 to 30), **Folder** (the path, with `~` for the home folder from `short_home_path`; an open button that uses `Gtk.FileLauncher`; and **Change…**, a `Gtk.FileDialog` folder choice), and **Default folder** with **Use**, which shows only while the form has a chosen folder. **Apply** posts the four settings and shows `Saved.`; the reply fills the form. Under **Backups**, the page shows the next backup (`backup_next_text`; while the page shows, `backup_refresh_delay` reads the status again 5 s after the next backup time, 1 s after local midnight, or every 60 s while a backup is late), the last failure, the files of this machine (for example `Today at 03:00 · 1.2 MB`), **Back up now** (it keeps unsaved form edits and shows `Saved the usage of <n> machines (<h> hours) to <file>.`), **Export…** (`Gtk.FileDialog.save`; `export_usage_file` writes the `GET /api/usage/export` document through a `0600` temporary file and `os.replace`), and **Import…** (filters `*.json` and all files; `import_usage_file` refuses a file over 64 MiB or a file that is not a JSON object, and posts it to `/api/usage/import`). Both file jobs run in a thread. `request_json` sends the source token on every `/api/usage/*` route, needs no `changed` key in their POST replies, waits 120 s for the export, the import, and **Back up now**, and turns a 404 into `This ASB engine has no usage backups. Update ASB, then try again.` The pure helpers before the GTK import are `short_home_path` (also used by `tooltip_model`), `backup_status_valid`, `backup_when`, `backup_size`, `backup_next_text`, `backup_error_text`, `count_text`, `backup_made_text`, `usage_import_text`, `backup_settings_body`, `usage_export_name`, `export_usage_file`, and `import_usage_file`.

In a narrow Preferences window (`max-width: 600px`), the `self.pages` stack shows the section list (`section_list`, one `Adw.ActionRow` for each section) in place of the sidebar and the page. `show_section(name)` opens a page with **Back** and the section name in the header title (`Adw.WindowTitle`); `go_back`, and Escape on a section page, show the list again with the focus on the row of that section. `navigation_changed` (`notify::current-breakpoint`) shows the list each time the narrow layout starts and the section page when it ends, and moves the focus from a hidden sidebar, list, or **Back** to the row of the current section. While the list shows, no section counts as visible: the Profiles, Phone, Machines, and Usage editors stop, and the UI report sends Profiles as not visible. In the narrow layout, `present_dialog` sets the `content_height` of the profile `Adw.Dialog` to the Preferences height, and to -1 (the natural height) when wide. In custom colors, `button.destructive-action` keeps the GNOME destructive color as its text color.

`asb group status [--json] | invite | join <link> [--name N] [--yes] | remove <name> | leave | reset-identity` and `asb network status [--json] | enable <address>... | disable` (`scripts/asb-group.mjs`) use these routes and `/api/phone` through `service.json`. `group status` prints one more last line from `relay`, only when the reply has it (the group has another machine): `Message relay: running.`, `Message relay: not running.` (`not-running`), `Message relay: not running. Bun was not found on PATH or in <folders>.` (`no-bun`), or `Message relay: stopped after 5 restarts in 10 minutes.` (`stopped`). `--json` prints the field as the engine sent it. `join` prints the inviter name and fingerprint from the link and asks `Does the other machine show this fingerprint? [y/N]` unless `--yes`. `reset-identity` refuses while the engine runs or while `group.json` exists, then deletes only `phone-tls/key.pem` and `phone-tls/cert.pem`.

### Message relay

Linux and Windows (plan 9.6, 9.7, 16, 17.6). `createRelaySupervisor` in `src/machine-relay.mjs` runs `bun bridge/dist/relay.js` while all are true: Linux or Windows, `bridge/dist/relay.js` and the placeholder (`cat` on `PATH`; Windows `%SystemRoot%\System32\findstr.exe`) exist, the group has another active member, and `findBun` in `src/platform-paths.mjs` finds Bun. `findBun` tries `bunOnPath` first (`PATH`; on Windows only `bun.exe`, because Node.js cannot start a `.cmd` file, such as the npm shim `bun.cmd`, without a shell), then `<BUN_INSTALL>/bin/bun` (Windows `bun.exe`) when `BUN_INSTALL` is an absolute path, then `$HOME/.bun/bin/bun` (Windows `%USERPROFILE%\.bun\bin\bun.exe`, from `env.USERPROFILE`). A candidate counts only when it is a regular file (and executable on Linux); tests inject the file check (`check`). The folder search is necessary because the engine of the Windows sign-in start (HKCU Run, tray) can have only the machine `Path` (Windows did not apply a long user `Path` on the test PC), and a systemd user service has a short `PATH`, while the Bun installer adds its folder only to the user `Path`. With `PATH` alone, Bun was not found, the relay never started, and `peerPeers` answered `[]`. `launch` uses the same lookup. When every other condition holds and `findBun` finds nothing, `wanted` logs one line, `ASB cannot start the message relay: Bun was not found on PATH or in <folders>. Messages to and from other machines do not work.` (`bunNotFound`, `bunFolders`). It logs again only after Bun was found and then lost again (`bunLogged`). With no other member, no `relay.js`, or no placeholder, it logs nothing. `status()` of the supervisor gives `{ state }`: `running`, `stopped` (after `RELAY_STOPPED`), `no-bun` (with `bunFolders`), or `not-running`. It checks at engine start, on each group change, and every 30 s, and stops the relay when the condition ends (Linux: SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 2 s; Windows: the end of stdin, then a kill after 2 s). The relay gets `ASB_ENGINE_URL`, `ASB_SOURCE_TOKEN`, and `ASB_RELAY_DIR` (`relay` in the runtime folder; Windows `%LOCALAPPDATA%\asb\run\relay`) and an open stdin; it exits when stdin ends. Its first stdout line within 10 s must be `{"port":<port>,"token":"<64 hex>"}`, else the engine kills it. A nonzero exit restarts it, at most 5 times in 10 minutes; then the engine logs `ASB: the message relay stopped after 5 restarts in 10 minutes.` and waits until the condition ends and returns or the engine restarts. The engine calls the relay on `127.0.0.1` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and one total deadline (`relayRequest`: 3 s for `GET /peers`, 8 s for `POST /deliver`, the member deadline for `POST /deliver-bound`; an HTTP error has its `status`), also when the relay sends headers and then stops. While the relay runs, the engine reads `GET /peer/v1/peers` of each other active member that its poller says is online every 5 s and keeps the last valid list (at most 256 entries; invalid entries are dropped). A member is offline when its poller says offline or its last list is older than 15 s.

| Loopback relay route | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/relay/homes` | `{ machineId, machineName, homes: [{ path, remoteClaude, hasRemoteControl }] }`. Homes are the parent of `projectsDir` of each enabled Claude source, then `~/.claude` and an absolute `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. Only existing folders count; the first home of each real `sessions` folder wins (`<real home>/sessions` is the key when that folder does not exist), with its real path and the `remoteClaude` of its first source (`auto` for the two extra homes). `hasRemoteControl` is true when the live registry of the home has a record with `bridgeSessionId` that is not a proxy row. |
| `GET /api/relay/remote-peers` | `{ peers: [{ machineId, machineName, online, sessionId, name, agent, status, remoteControl }] }` of the online members. |
| `POST /api/relay/send` | The relay body with the target `machineId`; `sender.machineId` must be this machine (else 400). Body limit 2 MiB. A target that is not another active member gives `refused`; an offline member gives `machine-offline` at once. Else `POST /peer/v1/deliver` to the member with a 10 s total timeout, first to the poller's last good address. A failure before the request went out gives `machine-offline`. A failure after it (timeout, reset, a reply that cannot be read) gives `unknown`. HTTP 400, 401, 403, or 410 (route checks before the relay) gives `refused`. Another status, or a 200 reply of another shape, gives `unknown`. A valid reply `socket-written`, `too-large`, or `rate-limited` passes unchanged; `target-unavailable`, `refused`, and `machine-offline` from the member give `unknown`, because a v1 member also sends them when it is not sure. `unknown` is only in this loopback reply, never on the member wire. |
| `POST /api/relay/receipt` | Only when the supervisor has `sendReceipt`. Body limit 64 KiB. The body is exactly `{ machineId, control }`: `machineId` is 16 hex characters, and `control` has the v1 control rules (`validControl`). Another body gives 400. `sendReceipt` selects the member by machine ID only (`others()`); no member or an offline poller gives no request. Else exactly one control `POST /peer/v2/deliver` (`{ api: 2, control }`, 4 s total, first to the poller's last good address), and no `/peer/v1/*` call. The reply of the member is not used. `200 { sent }`: `sent` is true when the request went out. |

The loopback relay routes need the source token, the local Host, and the local Origin for POST, and have `no-store`. The phone gateway never forwards them.

The relay process (`bridge/src/relay.ts`) binds `127.0.0.1` on a free port and accepts only its bearer token and its own `Host`. At start it removes the `*.sock` files in its folder and the proxy rows (`entrypoint: 'asb-relay'`) whose placeholder process is gone. `GET /peers` lists the live local sessions of all homes without proxy rows, with no socket paths or PIDs. Every 5 s it reads the homes and the remote peers from the engine and applies `proxyPlan`: a Codex session always gets a proxy row; a Claude session gets one with `bridge`, none with `native`, and with `auto` unless both sides have Claude Remote Control. A session that delivered to a local session keeps its reverse row for 30 minutes while it is listed, and for 120 s after its last delivery while it is not listed yet. Each home has at most 32 proxy rows. A proxy row is `<home>/sessions/<pid>.json` (`0600`) for a `cat` placeholder (found on `PATH`) that exits with the relay and gets the environment of the relay without `ASB_SOURCE_TOKEN` (Bun gives a child the environment of the process start, so the relay passes an explicit `env`), with `entrypoint: 'asb-relay'`, `cwd: ''`, no `notify_idle`, the name `<machine>-<session name>` (lowercase, at most 64 characters, `-2`, `-3` when taken), and a socket in the relay folder. A receipt for a held message from another machine goes to its proxy row, also when a Bridge process without the receiver (the MCP server or a hook) makes the decision: the outbox of the receiver finds the row by its socket path and `procStart`, checks the session ID, and takes only control frames for a proxy row. A frame on a proxy socket from a verified local session goes to `POST /api/relay/send` (30 s total). Only a proved not-sent result (`machine-offline`, `too-large`, `rate-limited`, `refused`, no engine at the port, or an engine 4xx) comes back to the sender as an `expired`/`refused` receipt with a short reason. With `unknown`, an older engine's `target-unavailable`, a reply of another shape, an engine 5xx, or a timeout or reset after the request went out, the relay sends no receipt and logs one line with the message ID only. Receipts forwarded to another machine stay fire and forget. `POST /deliver` writes the frame to the one live local session with the target ID, from the reverse row of the sender. `POST /deliver-bound` (strict body `{ binding, sender, message }`, else 400) writes one exact member send: the home whose real `sessions` folder is `registryHome` and `boundPeer` must still match every bound field, else `target-changed` with nothing written and no row made; then the reverse row (a failure gives `target-unavailable`), the frame (`too-large`), and `sendBound` (its refusal code, `receiver-stopped` as `relay-unavailable`, and the burst budget as `rate-limited`; another error gives `unknown`). The reply is `{ outcome: 'submitted' | 'unknown' }` or `{ outcome: 'not-sent', reason }`. It never searches by session ID and never calls the engine. `POST /outbox` (strict body `{ configDir, messagingSocketPath, request: { exactReceipt } }` with an absolute `configDir`, else 400) writes the receipt of an exact member send for a Windows engine: the receiver is only the `pid` and `procStart` of the receipt and the given path, with no registry read, and `requestReceiver` with the proof reads its key file in `configDir`. A failure of the path, the key file, or the proof before the first byte gives `{ outcome: 'not-sent' }`; a strict `{ applied }` reply passes; any other result gives `{ outcome: 'unknown' }`. Before the write the relay records the message ID with `{ rowKey, machineId, pid, procStart, messagingSocketPath, atMs }`: the key of the reverse row, the sending machine, and the bound target process. A not-sent write deletes the entry. It keeps the last 1024 exact message IDs with no age limit (the oldest out first). When `removeRow` removes a reverse row, the entries of the row stay but can no longer be valid, so their IDs stay exact. For each `peer_message_status` frame from a verified local writer, `receiptParts` splits the IDs (`orig_msg_id`, and `dropped_msg_ids` when `status` is `dropped`) into exact IDs (in the map) and legacy IDs. An exact ID is valid only when its entry has the row of the frame, the writer has the entry's pid, start time, and socket, and the entry is less than 30 minutes old. The valid IDs of one sending machine (the machine of the first valid ID) go fire and forget to `POST /api/relay/receipt` as `{ machineId, control }`, with the cuts of the legacy forward. An exact ID that is not valid, or of another sending machine, is dropped. The legacy IDs keep the legacy forward; when `orig_msg_id` is exact, the first legacy ID of `dropped_msg_ids` becomes `orig_msg_id`, and with no legacy ID there is no legacy call. An exact ID never takes the legacy path. A target `/clear` keeps the process, so its receipts still pass. All other frames, also a new user message from the target, keep the legacy path. When the engine does not answer for 30 s, the relay removes its rows. On SIGTERM, SIGINT, or the end of stdin it removes its rows and sockets. On Windows a proxy row has a `findstr.exe x` placeholder, a `cc-msg` named pipe, and a key file that the relay writes before the row and removes with it (also for stale rows at start); its inbox needs the `auth` line. The relay logs only short English error lines, never message text.

Codex senders (plan 16.4): `bridge/src/engine.ts` reads `service.json` from the first of these folders that passes the checks: `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/asb` (else `/run/user/<uid>/asb`), then `asb/run` in `XDG_STATE_HOME` when absolute, else in `$HOME/.local/state`. It uses the `readServiceRuntime` rules (private folder, owned `0600` file, exact keys, live PID); on Windows it reads `%LOCALAPPDATA%\asb\run\service.json` with no owner or mode checks, but it refuses links and calls the engine with the source token (Origin on POST; 2 s timeout, 12 s for `send`; reply limit 1 MiB). `bridge/.mcp.json` passes `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and `LOCALAPPDATA` to the MCP server. OpenCode uses the same `tools.ts` path with the OpenCode host (`bridge/src/opencode.ts`): `list_sessions` reports `agent: 'opencode'` for local OpenCode rows and `agent: 'claude'` for remote rows (the Q3 wire label), and its `from-mode` comes from the V27 rules of the first agent. It reads the OpenCode 2.1.x rules (`action`, `resource`, `effect`): `*` matches any action, and the last matching rule wins. A Claude Code session in bypass mode holds a message with no `from-mode`. `list_sessions` adds the entries of `GET /api/relay/remote-peers` after the local ones (`remote: true`, `machine`, name `<machine>-<name>`, no `cwd` or `startedAt`); an engine error gives the local list only. In parallel it calls `POST /api/agent/list-references` (`listReferences`, 2 s) with its thread ID and the absolute `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. The bridge drops each item that is not valid and keeps at most 256 items per list; an error, a refusal, another pid, or another shape gives no references. Each entry with an item gets exactly one of `reference` or `referenceUnavailable`, after its other fields. A remote entry whose `sessionId` is the task or a local entry is shown only with a `reference`, because a `sessionId` send goes to the local session. Without references, the output is the same as before. `send_message` to a `sessionId` with exactly one local non-proxy row sends locally and does not ask the engine. With no local row, the engine's remote list must have the ID on exactly one other machine: two or more machines give `More than one other machine has session <id>. Nothing sent.`, and a list error gives `ASB cannot list the sessions on the other machines. Nothing sent.` Then `GET /api/relay/homes` for this machine's ID (an error here gives `not-sent`), then `POST /api/relay/send` with the relay body (sender `agent: 'codex'`, the receiver row name, the mapped mode, and the same hop chain as a local send). The message row has the address and start time of the local proxy row of that session when one exists, so a receipt through it updates the status. `machine-offline`, `too-large`, `rate-limited`, and `refused` give `not-sent` with a `status_reason` and "Nothing sent". The engine reason `unknown`, an older engine's `target-unavailable`, an engine error, or a reply of another shape give `unknown` and `Send outcome unknown for <id>; inspect status before sending again`. The bridge never sends again by itself. `notify_when_idle` to a remote session is refused.

Exact local sends (Linux and Windows): `send_message` takes exactly one of `sessionId` and `reference` (`asb:` and printable ASCII, at most 384 characters); the bridge does not parse the reference. The advertised schema has a plain string for both, and the handler checks the format (`uuid`, `referenceText`) before it reads the caller or sends anything: a bad value gives the tool error `Input validation error: ... Nothing sent.`. `list_sessions` takes the optional `nameContains` (1 to 128 characters, case-insensitive, on the name that the list shows), leaves out null fields (a remote row has no `cwd` and no `startedAt`), and writes `startedAt` in whole seconds while it sorts on the milliseconds. `sendReference` in `bridge/src/bridge.ts` checks the text-or-notify rule and its own receiver, makes the message or subscription ID (`msgId`), and calls `POST /api/agent/resolve-bound` (5 s) with its thread ID, the absolute `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and `hostCheck: true`. No engine, an engine error, a reply of another shape, a reply `pid` that is not the engine pid, or another `msgId` gives `The local ASB engine did not answer. Nothing sent (engine-unavailable).`; an engine refusal gives `<message> Nothing sent (<code>).`. A binding for the task itself is refused, and a binding that no registry record matches gives `target-changed`. Then `sendLocal` (the same steps as a local `sessionId` send) writes the rows and the frames with the bound record. The receiver outbox takes the bound request `{ binding, frames }` (strict, no `sessionId` key): `sendBound` in `bridge/src/outbound.ts` refuses an expired binding (`binding-expired`), and frames whose first `msg_id` is not `msgId` or that name another session, or a registry that has no single non-proxy record with the bound real registry root, session ID, pid, start time, and socket (`target-changed`). It checks the same again after the connect and before the first byte, with `receiver-stopped` when the receiver closes. On Windows, the bridge sends `pipeProof: true`, and the write to its own receiver outbox and the write to the target each go only through a pipe handle whose server it proved (the bound PID, its `procStart`, and the same Windows user); a failed proof sends nothing and gives `receiver-stopped` or `target-changed`. These refusals, a failed connect to the outbox, and the burst limit leave the row `not-sent`. Only a failure after the handoff, or a lost or malformed outbox reply, gives `unknown`. This path never calls `findPeer`, `remotePeers`, or `/api/relay/*`, so a reference never falls back to a session ID or another machine. Receipts and idle notices keep the rules of a local send (the peer address and start time), so they still match after a `/clear`. `notify_when_idle` with a reference of this machine has the local rules: a target with no `notify_idle` refuses the whole call before any row or frame. Not in this step: `claude-remote` and `opencode` targets, and macOS. A `claude-local` target is a Claude Desktop Code session: the engine finds it in the registry by `hostSessionId` (the `resolve-bound` paragraph), puts that ID in the binding, and the bridge checks `hostSessionId` again before the first byte (`target-changed` when it does not match). An older ASB Bridge that sends no `hostCheck: true` gets `host-check-required`.

Exact member sends (a Linux or Windows sender and target, Codex senders, `codex`, `claude-cli`, and `claude-local` targets): the platform check of `memberTarget` passes on Linux and Windows, with no `pipeProof`, because the bridge writes no pipe in this path. When `resolve-bound` refuses with `member-target`, `sendReference` refuses `notify_when_idle` (`Idle subscriptions do not work across machines yet. Nothing sent (remote-idle-unsupported).`, no row), else `sendMember` in `bridge/src/bridge.ts` checks the frame size (with any UUID as the session ID), inserts the row (`peer_id` = the reference, no peer address or start time, `submitting`), and calls `POST /api/agent/send-member` (16 s) with the caller, the mapped mode, the message (`msgId`, text, and the hop chain of a local send), and `remainingMs: 10000`. A strict reply with the engine pid and the same `msgId` maps `submitted` to `socket-written` and `not-sent` to `not-sent` with `status_reason` `<message> Nothing sent (<code>).`; anything else (a throw, another shape, pid, or `msgId`) gives `unknown` and `Send outcome unknown for <id>; inspect status before sending again`. It never calls `peers()`, `findPeer`, `remotePeers`, or `/api/relay/*`, and never sends again by itself. `sendExact` in `src/machine-relay.mjs` selects the member by the reference's machine ID only (`others()`, not the relay lists, so the sending machine does not need its relay): none gives `unknown-machine` and an offline poller gives `machine-offline`, with no request. Then one `POST /peer/v2/resolve-session` (4 s, first to the poller's last good address). All resolve results are not sent: a failure before the request went out gives `machine-offline`; 404 gives `peer-upgrade-required`; 400, 401, 403, 410, or 429 give `refused`; a valid `unavailable` gives its reason; a failure after the request went out, another status, or another shape gives `capability-unknown`. After a valid `ready`: `deliverMs = min(10000, remainingMs)`, where 1000 or less gives `binding-expired` with no request; a body that fails the v2 check gives `invalid-request`. Then exactly one `POST /peer/v2/deliver` (timeout `deliverMs`, body `remainingMs: deliverMs - 1000`, first to the address of the resolve reply): a failure before the request went out gives `machine-offline`; a failure after it gives `unknown`; 404 gives `peer-upgrade-required`; 400, 401, 403, 410, or 429 give `refused`; 413 gives `too-large`; a valid v2 reply with the same `msgId` passes; another status or shape gives `unknown`. A failure never starts a v1 request, a second deliver, or a request to another member. On the member, `bindMemberTarget` in `src/session-targets.mjs` has no caller registry: the `resolveSessionReference` failures pass through, another machine ID gives `unknown-machine`, then the platform (Linux or Windows), type, `source-disabled`, and `not-found` checks of `resolve-bound` (with no host-check step), and then the binding in the homes of the target source only: for `claude-cli` and `claude-local` the source home (`dirname(projectsDir)`), for `codex` the homes that the relay serves (`machineRelay.homes`). Each home must have a private registry (none gives `not-live`). The candidates come from all these homes (more than one gives `ambiguous-identity`), and `registryHome` is the root of the registry that has the candidate. The target needs the legacy reverse row of the sender (`from`), with the legacy rules. On Windows the relay (`sendBound`) proves the target pipe before the write.

Receipts of exact member sends (Linux and Windows): only the target's own receipt frame makes a receipt; ASB never makes one. `memberTarget` also gives the internal `returnTo` (`runtimeSessionId`, `pid`, `procStart`, `messagingSocketPath` of the caller record, and the caller `configDir`), which only `sendExact` gets. `sendExact` keeps its attempts in memory, by `msgId`: `{ certFingerprint, reference, returnTo, expiresAtMs, controls }`, with no text, name, hop chain, or mode. It keeps at most 1024 attempts (the oldest out first) for 30 minutes. A `msgId` that already has an attempt gives `invalid-request` with no request. The attempt is saved just before the deliver request and deleted on a not-sent result. On the target machine the relay sends the valid exact IDs to `POST /api/relay/receipt`, and the engine sends one control `POST /peer/v2/deliver` to the sending member. There, `peerReceipt` uses the control only when the attempt of `orig_msg_id` exists, has not expired, has the caller's full certificate fingerprint, and has had at most 4 controls. Each failed check gives the same reply, not-sent `binding-expired`, with no write, so the route does not show which message IDs exist. For `dropped`, an ID of `dropped_msg_ids` is used only when its attempt passes the same checks (without the count) and has the same `reference` and `returnTo`. Then on Linux `writeOutbox` writes one request `{ exactReceipt: { runtimeSessionId, pid, procStart, reference, msgIds, status, status_detail?, reason?, drop_reason? } }` to the outbox of the sending task's receiver: the inbox path with `.sock` replaced by `.out`, absolute, with no `..`, a socket of this user with no group or other mode bits in a folder with the same rules. It writes one line and reads one line of at most 64 KiB, `{ applied: <integer> }`, in 2 s total. A reply gives `submitted`; a failure before the connect gives not-sent `target-unavailable`; a failure after the write gives `unknown`. The receiver's outbox (`bridge/src/outbound.ts`) takes `exactReceipt` as one more strict request. The receiver applies it only when `runtimeSessionId` is its thread ID and `pid` and `procStart` are its own process (the start time of `start()`); else `applied: 0`. In one transaction it updates each ID whose row is `direction='out'`, has the reference as `peer_id` and no peer address, and is `submitting`, `socket-written`, `unknown`, or `held`, with the status mapping of `receive()` (`expired` with `status_detail: 'refused'` gives `refused`). A receipt can move an `unknown` row to its state, because it proves that the frame came to the target. A final state stays. There is no local budget change. The incarnation is strict: after a sender restart or reload the receipt is lost and the row keeps its state. A receipt is also lost when the legacy reverse row on the target machine has ended, when its attempt has expired, or when a step in between fails. A lost receipt leaves the row `socket-written` (submitted). The receipt of a Windows target goes back to the sender the same way. On Windows the sending engine gives the receipt to its relay with `POST /outbox` (`relayOutbox`, 2 s, body `{ configDir, messagingSocketPath, request }`), because Node cannot prove a pipe server and the engine reads no key file. The relay writes it through a proved pipe to the receiver outbox `\\.\pipe\LOCAL\asb-out-<32 hex>`. `{ applied }` gives `submitted`. No relay, HTTP 400, 401, or 404 (an older relay), or `{ outcome: 'not-sent' }` give not-sent `target-unavailable`. Another reply, another status, or a timeout gives `unknown`. Without the relay (Bun on the engine's `PATH`) the receipt is lost and the row stays `socket-written`.

Delivery limits nest, so an inner step answers before its caller stops waiting:

| Step | Limit |
| --- | --- |
| Bridge `POST /api/relay/send` (`engineRequest`); relay proxy call to the engine | 12 s; 30 s |
| Bridge `POST /api/agent/resolve-bound` (`engineRequest`); life of the binding (`expiresAtMs`) | 5 s; 10 s |
| Engine `POST /peer/v1/deliver` to the member (`peerRequest`) | 10 s total for all addresses |
| Receiver engine to its relay (`relayRequest` `POST /deliver`) | 8 s total |
| Relay write to the target (`sendFrames`) | 5 s total for connect and write, then the 150 ms flush |
| Bridge `POST /api/agent/send-member` (`engineRequest`) | 16 s; body `remainingMs: 10000` (the delivery budget) |
| Engine `POST /peer/v2/resolve-session` (its own deadline) | 4 s total for all addresses |
| Engine `POST /peer/v2/deliver` | `min(10 s, remainingMs - local work)` total |
| v2 body `remainingMs` | deliver timeout - 1 s |
| Member deadline | `min(remainingMs - 500 ms, 8 s)` |
| Binding `expiresAtMs` of an exact member send | `min(10 s life, member deadline - 5.5 s)` |
| Member to relay `POST /deliver-bound` | member deadline - now |
| Relay `POST /api/relay/receipt` | 30 s total (the engine call of the relay) |
| Engine control `POST /peer/v2/deliver` | 4 s total, one POST |
| Outbox write of an exact receipt | 2 s total |
| Attempt life on the sending engine; exact receipt window of the target relay | 30 min |
| Exact receipts | At most 1024 attempts, 4 controls for each attempt |

Mixed versions: a new bridge with an old engine, or a new sender engine with an old member, can still report an outcome in the old way (for example "Nothing sent" after a timeout). Only a new bridge with a new sender engine gives the honest outcomes. The v1 member wire stays as it is; v2 has only `POST /peer/v2/resolve-session` and `POST /peer/v2/deliver`. For an exact member send, a member without v2 gives `peer-upgrade-required` before any text goes out, and a member engine with an older `relay.js` gives `relay-unavailable` (see `bridge/PROTOCOL-COVERAGE.md`). Exact receipts need the new bridge and engine on the sending machine and the new engine and `relay.js` on the target. With an older part the receipt is lost and the row stays submitted: an older target relay drops exact receipts, an older sender engine answers the control with 400, an older bridge answers the outbox request with `{ error }`, and an older target engine answers `/api/relay/receipt` with 404.

## Local writes

| Default location | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `~/.local/state/asb/pending.json` | ASB attention, pins, Persistent unread, Discard marks (current-task and next-task), drawer marks, and Mute. |
| `~/.local/state/asb/palette.json` | The last palette from the window, for the dashboard `palette` field, mode `0600`. The server reads it at start. A bad file gives `palette: null` and one stderr warning; the next publish replaces it. |
| `~/.config/asb/layout.json` | Width, view, optional `cardLimit` (50, 100, 200, 500, or 0 for All; default 100, not written when 100), and optional `copyKind` (`absolute`; absent means Relative). `read_layout` still accepts a `showAppPills` key with any value and ignores it; `write_layout` no longer writes it. |
| `~/.config/asb/theme.json` | Custom colors. |
| `~/.config/asb/activity-windows.json` | The sizes of the Messages and Usage windows: `{ version: 1, messages: { width, height, maximized }, usage: {...} }`, width and height from 360 to 16384. Written when one of these windows closes. A missing or bad file gives the default sizes. |
| `~/.cache/asb/usage/claude-message-cache.json` | The Claude message scan state, saved after a Messages read that changed it, at most once in 5 minutes: SHA-256 path keys, file size, inode, device, change times, first-byte hash, read offset, failed-send IDs, and message IDs, directions, times, statuses, body hashes, and line offsets and lengths. No path, name, title, folder name, or message text. Mode `0600` in a `0700` folder. A broken or old-version file is ignored. You can delete it at any time. |
| `~/.cache/asb/usage/usage-cache.json.gz` | The gzip-compressed usage scan cache, after the first usage scan (an idle Usage request or member activity reply when due, the first request of a new install, an export, or the ledger timer due 10 minutes after the engine start, which waits for idle for at most an hour); busy requests use the saved ledger totals: per transcript a SHA-256 of its path, its size and change time, the read offset, a hash of its first 64 bytes, the reader state, and the token records (quarter hour, model name, four token counts, and a 22-character digest of the IDs that count a record once: the Claude message and request IDs, the Grok session and prompt IDs, or the Pi entry ID). No message text, path, title, or folder name. Version 2. The newest 10,000 files, with no age limit. Mode `0600` in a `0700` folder. |
| `~/.cache/asb/usage/usage-model-rates.json` | The LiteLLM rates of the last download: the download time and the four rates (input, output, cache read, cache write) of each model. Mode `0600` in a `0700` folder. |
| `~/.config/asb/usage/usage-ledger.json` | The usage ledger, after the first usage scan of the engine: `{ version: 1, self: { machineId, name, records }, machines: [{ machineId, name, exportedAtMs, records }] }`, each record `[hourMs, app, model, uncached, cacheRead, cacheWrite, output]` (the UTC hour start), sorted by hour, app, and model. The hourly token usage of this machine and of the imported machines; no message text, path, title, folder name, or cost. Mode `0600` in a `0700` folder. A bad file moves to `usage-ledger.<ms>.bad.json`. Uninstall does not remove it. See [Usage ledger](#usage-ledger). |
| `~/.config/asb/usage/usage-backup.json` | The usage backup settings, after the first save: `{ version: 1, enabled, time, keep, folder }`. Mode `0600` in a `0700` folder. Uninstall does not remove it. See [Usage backups](#usage-backups). |
| `~/.config/asb/usage/backups/` | The daily usage backups `asb-usage-<name>-<id8>-<YYYY-MM-DD>.json` (export documents), mode `0600` in a `0700` folder; the newest `keep` files of this machine stay. A folder that the user chose keeps its own mode. Uninstall does not remove them. |
| `~/.config/asb/sources.json` | App-source IDs, labels, colors, dot visibility, paths, launchers, and enabled flags, with owner-only access. |
| `~/.config/asb/phone.json` | Phone access settings and paired devices with token SHA-256 values, mode `0600`. An invalid file moves to `phone.json.bad`. |
| `~/.config/asb/phone-tls/` | Phone certificate `cert.pem` and key `key.pem`, mode `0600`, folder `0700`. The certificate is also the machine identity. |
| `~/.config/asb/group.json` | Machine group: members, tombstones, and group phones with token SHA-256 values, mode `0600`. An invalid file moves to `group.json.bad`. |
| `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/asb/service.json` | Shared desktop/service descriptor: schema, pid, port, and per-run source token, mode `0600`, user-owned folder `0700`. Without an absolute `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`: `service.json` in `asb/run` in `XDG_STATE_HOME` when it is absolute, else in `~/.local/state`. |
| `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/asb/stopped.json` | Explicit engine quit marker: exactly `schema` and `token` (the SHA-256 hex of the source token). The reader also accepts the form of ASB 1.11.0, which has `pid` too, and ignores the PID, so a newer window closes at once when a 1.11.0 engine quits. It uses the same folder and file checks as `service.json`, including its fallback and Windows paths. The engine writes it only for `POST /api/engine/stop`, before it removes `service.json`. |
| Windows: `%APPDATA%\asb`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\asb\state`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\asb\run`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\asb\cache` | The same files as the config, state, and runtime folders (the board's `theme.json`, `layout.json`, and `activity-windows.json`, and `usage\usage-ledger.json`, `usage\usage-backup.json`, and the `usage\backups` folder, are in the config folder), plus `engine.log` in `state`. The phone certificate is `cert.pfx`, `pfx-pass`, and `cert.cer`. `cache` holds `readers\positions.json`, `usage\usage-cache.json.gz`, `usage\usage-model-rates.json`, `board-check.json`, and the compiled board code in `pycache`. The four folders are limited to the user with `icacls`. |
| Windows: `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run` value `ASB` | The tray command, only after `asb engine install` or `asb setup`. ASB never changes the firewall. |
| Windows: `ASB.lnk` in the Start menu Programs folder | The Start menu link, only after `asb engine install` or `asb setup`. Its description is `Agent Switch Board (generated by ASB)`, the marker. `asb engine uninstall` removes only a link with this marker. |
| `~/.local/share/gnome-shell/extensions/asb@jamir-boop.github.io/` | The top-bar extension files, only from the per-user install on GNOME Shell 45 or later. The install record has the SHA-256 of each file (`panel/<name>`); the install and uninstall change only files with a matching record, and leave an extension that ASB did not write. |
| GSettings `org.gnome.shell enabled-extensions` | The per-user install adds `asb@jamir-boop.github.io` only when it adds the extension (`asb --package install`, not the tests). It never changes `disabled-extensions`; uninstall changes neither. |
| `~/.config/systemd/user/asb.service` | User service unit, only after `asb service install`. |
| `~/.config/autostart/asb-engine.desktop` | XDG autostart entry, only after `asb service install` in `xdg` mode. |
| `~/.local/share/applications/local.asb.AgentSwitchBoard.desktop` | Per-user launcher. |
| `~/.local/share/icons/hicolor/scalable/apps/local.asb.AgentSwitchBoard.svg` | Per-user app icon. |

XDG state/config/data overrides apply. The per-user package also installs ASB under its own local data folder and adds `~/.local/bin/asb`. `~/.local/bin/asb` records `ASB_NODE` and starts the shipped `scripts/asb`. It keeps `ASB_NODE` when it is an absolute, executable regular file with no control characters; otherwise it records the current Node executable. Paths use shell quoting. If the recorded Node.js is not usable, `scripts/asb` tries `node` on `PATH`, `~/.local/bin/node`, `/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/node`, `/usr/local/bin/node`, then `/usr/bin/node`, and uses the first that is Node.js 20 or later with SQLite. It prints a message with a link to the README `Node.js` section if none is usable. The launcher records `ASB_PYTHON` only when `checkRuntime` found a Python with PyGObject. ASB does not put system folders before the caller's `PATH`. It appends `/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin` and a recorded SQLite folder, so apps started from ASB keep the caller's `PATH` order. Uninstall removes the `asb/releases` and `asb` data folders when they are empty. SIGINT/SIGTERM abort package work, await pending writes and rollback, and remove only its acquired lock and staging folder. A foreign lock stays intact and the error identifies its folder. See the installer for its exact manifest. Original app stores stay read-only, with one exception: the read-only SQLite open of the Codex state database can create `-wal` and `-shm` side files next to it. The files `pending.json.bad`, `sources.json.bad`, `phone.json.bad`, and `group.json.bad` can appear next to their originals.

Only when the user runs them with targets, `asb bridge install` and `asb bridge uninstall` change Codex plugin configuration through the `codex` CLI and OpenCode plugin folders under the OpenCode config folder. They copy or remove the Codex `$bridge` and Claude Code `/bridge` skill folders and the OpenCode `bridge` skill. `--opencode` installs only the OpenCode plugin; `--opencode-config <path>` names the OpenCode config folder; `--all` adds OpenCode when that folder exists. Without `--opencode-config`, the OpenCode folders come from `opencode debug paths`, else from `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_STATE_HOME`, else from `~/.config/opencode` and `~/.local/state/opencode`. Before Codex changes anything, they check the Claude skill folder and the OpenCode plugin and skill folders, so a refused folder leaves no partial change. After an OpenCode uninstall, they print `Restart OpenCode to unload the ASB Bridge plugin.`, because OpenCode keeps the loaded plugin in memory. Without targets, they show usage and change nothing.

On Windows, close Codex before `asb bridge install` or `asb bridge uninstall` because these commands cannot change a Codex home while Codex runs (`Access is denied` or `being used by another process`). If this occurs, the command warns for that home, continues with the other targets, and exits 1. The warning tells you which command to run again.

`asb bridge status` shows Codex marketplace sources without Windows verbatim drive or UNC prefixes, in text and JSON. The installer compares the same normalized paths, so an unchanged source does not cause marketplace removal. Other paths stay unchanged. This uses the same path rule as Codex session folder display. The installer keeps a local copy of the rule so it can run without `src/`. For each Codex, Claude, and OpenCode skill, JSON includes `skillCurrent`: `true` if the installed skill is `ok` and its files and hashes match the package, `false` if it is `ok` but differs, or `null` for other states or a read error. Text shows `skill ok (current)` or `skill ok (out of date: run asb bridge install <target>)`, with `--claude`, `--opencode`, or `--codex-home <home>` as the target.

The optional bridge plugin runs inside Codex or OpenCode 2. The Codex plugin writes per-task state under `<CODEX_HOME>/plugin-state/asb-bridge/` and registry rows under `<Claude home>/sessions/` (on Windows also a key file with a random local token for each row). The Claude home is `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.claude`. The OpenCode plugin writes registry rows in the same Claude home, with `entrypoint` `opencode-asb-bridge` and an `opencodeSessionId` field; the row name is `opencode-<title>-<suffix>`, and the session ID is a UUID v5 of `opencode:<ses_...>`. It never edits `opencode.json`, the OpenCode database, or `auth.json`. Uninstall keeps the per-task message history. The revival sweep of the bridge (`hook.js --revive`) deletes the state file of a task, with its `-wal` and `-shm` files, only when the task ended more than 30 days ago, is not in the loaded-thread list of the app-server, and has no live receiver row; it deletes nothing when that list fails.

The OpenCode plugin's `setup()` returns an async cleanup function, or `undefined` when required APIs are missing. OpenCode calls this function when it releases the location. The native mock-only check uses `POST /api/location/reload` and proves that the old placeholder exits, the session registers with a new placeholder, and inbound delivery reaches the mock provider. A plugin file change alone is not a reload proof.

`asb bridge link-claude <profile-home> <shared-home>` is a separate opt-in write operation. It shares only the native `sessions` registry, which holds peer metadata and IPC authentication keys. It leaves settings, transcripts, app data, logins, provider credentials, Codex homes, and the ASB source registry separate. Both existing registry directories must be real, user-owned, and `0700`; owned `0755` and `0775` config homes are supported without a permission change. The command validates known JSON/key entries, checks conflicts before writes, copies with exclusive creation, keeps the original source in a private backup, and creates a directory link to the shared registry. A correct existing link is unchanged. Unknown files, path containment, unsafe paths, and changing source files cause refusal. Rollback restores the source when its path is free and removes only unchanged copies. A recreated source path or changed copy is kept and reported, with the backup. Install and uninstall keep the link and all registry data. Native Claude Code `2.1.303` follows this link and has no separate registry override; this internal behavior has no compatibility promise. See [the setup guide](bridge/README.md#link-claude-profiles).

## Checks and release

```bash
npm test
python3 test/asb_native_logic_test.py
npm run build
cd android && ./gradlew --no-daemon testDebugUnitTest lint assembleDebug assembleRelease
```

The first two commands do not launch the ASB window. `npm test` needs no `sqlite3` command and no environment variable. Launcher checks use isolated HOME/XDG/source folders, `ASB_SYSTEM_INSTALL=1`, and `ASB_PYTHON=/bin/true` to verify busy/free ports without GTK or icon registration. Display-free data checks share the synthetic GTK fixtures through AST extraction without importing GTK. Native widget tests are separate and need a GTK display with synthetic fixtures. Use them only when UI testing is in scope. Do not use real session stores for public media or test captures.

The top-bar extension has Node tests for its pure logic and its files (`test/gnome-shell-model.test.mjs`, `test/gnome-shell-files.test.mjs`); they never start GNOME Shell. The package tests inject the GNOME Shell version and the `gsettings` runner, so they never run either program. The extension itself is checked outside CI in a headless GNOME Shell (`gnome-shell --headless --virtual-monitor 1920x1080 --wayland --no-x11`) with a temporary HOME, private session and system buses that can activate no service, the keyfile GSettings backend, and a fake engine. The Android build needs JDK 21 and the Android SDK; see `android/README.md`. The phone gateway certificate tests skip when `openssl` is not on `PATH`. The service tests use a fake `systemctl` and do not change the real user session.

The root tests skip TypeScript files. After bridge changes, run `cd bridge && bun run check && bun test && bun run build`, and commit `bridge/dist/`. When you change a shipped bridge file, change the version in `bridge/package.json` and `bridge/.codex-plugin/plugin.json` together. Codex caches an installed plugin by version.

Native SSE retains its connection/header timeout but removes the live read timeout after validated stream headers; close shuts down blocked reads. Row ages use relative text for all elapsed days, and empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` falls back to the home config folder. Row and tooltip indicator CSS share the same shape rules and keep their existing roles/sizes.

Native RGB conversion limits extended GNOME color channels to `0..255` before it formats `#RRGGBB`. This keeps theme editor values and phone palettes valid. Native tests use `Cantarell 11` for exact pixel checks and a separate larger-font check for row packing; production font and minimum row sizes stay unchanged.

The source build (`npm run build`) produces `asb_1.13.0_all.deb`, `asb-1.13.0-1.noarch.rpm` (only when `rpmbuild` is installed: without it, the build prints one line and makes no RPM), `asb-1.13.0-linux.tar.gz`, a `PKGBUILD` with the SHA-256 of that archive, and `SHA256SUMS` (the hashes of the other files) in `dist/`. The `.deb` and the RPM have the same files. Both list the engine tools as dependencies and the board libraries as Recommends (weak dependencies), so that a release with an old GTK still installs the engine. The `.deb` has `Depends: nodejs (>= 20), sqlite3, xdg-utils, openssl` and `Recommends: python3-gi, python3-gi-cairo, gir1.2-gtk-4.0 (>= 4.12), gir1.2-adw-1 (>= 1.4), python3-qrcode, pipewire-bin | pulseaudio-utils | alsa-utils`. The RPM is one `noarch` package for the Fedora, RHEL, and openSUSE families, with rich dependencies: `Requires` for Node.js 20 or later (`nodejs`, `nodejs-default`, `nodejs22`, or `nodejs24`), `sqlite` or `sqlite3`, `openssl`, and `xdg-utils`, and `Recommends` for the board libraries with the names of both families, `(gobject-introspection if python3-gobject)`, `(libglvnd-gles if gtk4)`, the QR code, and the sound players. `AutoReqProv: no` stops `rpmbuild` from adding a Python ABI dependency, which would break engine only. The `PKGBUILD` takes the release archive as its source, with the SHA-256 of the archive that the same build made. It lists the board libraries as hard `depends`, because Arch always has current libraries, and `python-qrcode`, `python-cairo`, `pipewire-audio`, and `bun` as `optdepends`. The v1.13.0 release has all five files. They come from one CI build (the `build` job of the Distros workflow), because the RPM needs `rpmbuild`. The `.deb` uses xz on every host (`dpkg-deb -Zxz`): the `dpkg-deb` of Ubuntu makes zstd members by default, and a zstd `.deb` needs dpkg 1.21.18 or later. The flake works from the default branch now (`nix profile install github:Jamir-boop/ASB`), and `flake.lock` pins nixpkgs `nixos-26.05`; `nix/package.nix` wraps the launcher with `ASB_SYSTEM_INSTALL`, `ASB_INSTALLED_LAUNCHER`, `ASB_NODE` (Node.js 24), `ASB_PYTHON` (Python with PyGObject, pycairo, and `qrcode`), and the `PATH` of `xdg-utils`, `openssl`, `sqlite`, `pipewire`, and `alsa-utils`. Both packages install the command `/usr/bin/asb`, which sets `ASB_SYSTEM_INSTALL=1` and `ASB_INSTALLED_LAUNCHER=/usr/bin/asb`; the `PKGBUILD` and the Nix wrapper set the same two variables. The Nix flake (`flake.nix`, `nix/package.nix`) is in the repository only: `node scripts/asb-package.mjs stage <folder>` copies the runtime files (`RUNTIME_FILES`) to a folder for it, and it wraps them with Node.js, Python, GTK, and Libadwaita. The npm package ships no `nix/`, `flake.nix`, or `dist/`. The portable root is `asb-1.13.0/` with `./install.sh`. See [release notes](docs/releases/v1.13.0.md) for upgrades and check limits. Public demo assets use synthetic session names and folders. The current demo source shows four app switches; the README shows the SVG hero `docs/media/asb-hero.svg` instead of the video. Remotion build dependencies are separate from the ASB runtime.

The `1.9.0` release build passed 664 Node tests, 148 native logic tests, 29 GTK widget tests on a hidden X display, and 132 ASB Bridge tests on Linux. A Debian `apt install`, real apps, a machine group on real machines, a real phone, Windows, macOS, and FPS on the real display were not tested for that build. See [1.9.0 release notes](docs/releases/v1.9.0.md) for its check limits.

The `1.10.0` release build passed 767 Node tests (766 pass, 1 skipped), 166 native logic tests, 14 tray tests, 38 GTK widget tests on a hidden X display (GTK 4.18.6, Libadwaita 1.7.6), and 214 ASB Bridge tests with Bun (207 pass, 7 skipped), plus the bridge check with a stock Codex, on Linux. A local build of the release source made the `.deb`, the archive, and the `PKGBUILD`, and the 75 runtime files in the `.deb` and in the archive equal the source. CI passed on commit `35a1986`, the source before the `.deb` compression fix and the version change. The five release files come from the CI build of the release commit. Real Codex and Claude apps, a machine group on real machines, a real phone, macOS, and FPS on the real display were not tested for that build. See [1.10.0 release notes](docs/releases/v1.10.0.md) for its check limits.

The `1.11.1` release build passed 983 Node tests (981 pass, 2 skipped), 215 native logic tests, 14 tray tests, 77 GTK widget tests on a hidden X display (GTK 4.18.6, Libadwaita 1.7.6), 266 ASB Bridge tests with Bun (259 pass, 7 skipped), and 195 Android unit tests with Android lint, on Linux. CI passed on commit `db3f0c6`, the source before the version change. The five release files come from the CI build of the release commit. A Linux PC and a Windows PC of a machine group ran the changes while they were made, but not the release build. The OpenCode plugin changes ran only in the Bun tests, not with a real OpenCode. macOS and FPS on the real display were not tested for that build. See [1.11.1 release notes](docs/releases/v1.11.1.md) for its check limits.

The `1.12.0` release build passed 1038 Node tests (1036 pass, 2 skipped), 233 native logic tests, 14 tray tests, 81 GTK widget tests on a hidden X display (GTK 4.18.6, Libadwaita 1.7.6), and 266 ASB Bridge tests with Bun (259 pass, 7 skipped), on Linux. The Android app did not change after `1.11.1`. CI passed on commit `7b23613`, the source before the version change. The five release files come from the CI build of the release commit. A Linux PC and a Windows PC of a machine group ran the source before the version change. After the publication, they installed the release build (the published archive, and an npm pack of the tag), and both engines started. On the Windows PC (GTK 4.24.1, Libadwaita 1.10.0), Preferences > Profiles, the profile dialog, Preferences > Machines, and the Filters popover passed a check with made-up data. The new Profiles page and profile dialog were not tested with Libadwaita 1.4. Claude sessions that run in the cloud, macOS, and FPS on the real display were not tested for that build. See [1.12.0 release notes](docs/releases/v1.12.0.md) for its check limits.

The `1.12.1` release build passed 1046 Node tests (1044 pass, 2 skipped), 235 native logic tests, 14 tray tests, 83 GTK widget tests on a hidden X display (GTK 4.18.6, Libadwaita 1.7.6), 266 ASB Bridge tests with Bun (259 pass, 7 skipped), and 196 Android unit tests with lint, on Linux. CI passed on commit `5acb727`, the source before the version change, and its AlmaLinux 9 row opened Preferences > Profiles and the profile dialog with GTK 4.12.3 and Libadwaita 1.4.2. The five release files come from the CI build of the release commit. A Linux PC and a Windows PC of a machine group ran the runtime files of the source before the version change. After the publication, they installed the release build (the published archive, and an npm pack of the tag), and both engines started. On the Windows PC, `asb agent status` showed `UI: not-open` 0.8 s after a normal close of the board. The narrow Preferences window was not checked on Windows. Claude sessions that run in the cloud, macOS, and FPS on the real display were not tested for that build. See [1.12.1 release notes](docs/releases/v1.12.1.md) for its check limits.

The `1.13.0` release build passed 1420 Node tests (1418 pass, 2 skipped), 326 native logic tests, 21 tray tests, 247 GTK widget tests on a hidden X display (GTK 4.18.6, Libadwaita 1.7.6), 266 ASB Bridge tests with Bun (259 pass, 7 skipped), and 241 Android unit tests with lint, on Linux. The top-bar extension passed 225 checks in a headless GNOME Shell 48.7 with the Colloid shell theme. CI passed on commit `b63f9dd`, the source before the version change, and its AlmaLinux 9 row opened Preferences > Profiles and the profile dialog with GTK 4.12.3 and Libadwaita 1.4.2. The five release files come from the CI build of the release commit. A Linux PC and two Windows 10 PCs of a machine group ran the runtime files of the source before the version change, and each showed the other two online. After the publication, they installed the release build (the published archive, and an npm pack of the tag), and the three engines started. The test phone installed the Android build of the tag, and its live service started. GNOME Shell 45, 46, 47, and 49, the stock GNOME Shell theme, Preferences > Usage and the narrow Preferences window on Windows, Claude sessions that run in the cloud, macOS, and FPS on the real display were not tested for that build. See [1.13.0 release notes](docs/releases/v1.13.0.md) for its check limits.

The published `1.8.1` baseline passed 272 Node tests, 87 native logic tests, and one focused GTK toolbar test on an isolated hidden Xephyr display with Cairo and synthetic data. The full GTK widget suite did not run. Real profile edits were not tested. Resize lag on X11 remains unresolved. That release had no new FPS measurement. See [1.8.1 release notes](docs/releases/v1.8.1.md) for its check limits. Earlier performance samples and their limits remain in the [1.6.1 release notes](docs/releases/v1.6.1.md) and [1.6.0 release notes](docs/releases/v1.6.0.md).

The earlier unreleased Drawer build had thirteen GTK widget tests. An earlier unreleased Drawer build was tested on one X11 machine, before the performance changes. All thirteen tests passed on a nested test display for that earlier build. The desktop display run was made before the thirteenth test was added: eleven tests passed and the pointer test was skipped. One test uses real pointer clicks through `xdotool`. It runs only when `xdotool` is installed and `DISPLAY` is `:9` or `:10` (a nested test display), so that it cannot move the pointer of a desktop; in other cases it is skipped. The tests set the Adwaita icon theme, because their pixel checks read icon pixels and the highest alpha of an icon is different in each icon theme. Four of them had expectations older than the 1.3.0 layout and were repaired after the `1.5.0` release. The 68px Comfortable row assertions hold with that machine's font; with Adwaita Sans or Noto Sans the rows are 70px. Two tests check row packing with another font and with overlay scrolling off. For ASB `1.5.0`, card motion and the wheel spring were also measured on that display with synthetic rows. No Debian `apt install` was run. Native pixel fidelity remains unverified. See the [publication record](docs/OPEN_SOURCE_PLAN.md) for verified release checks.

### Linux distribution checks (CI)

`.github/workflows/distros.yml` runs on every push to any branch and on `workflow_dispatch`. It has 32 jobs: `probe` (15 rows), `build` (1), `check` (15 rows), and `nix` (1). The 15 rows are in the table. The `alma-9` rows (AlmaLinux 9 with GTK 4.12.3 and Libadwaita 1.4.2, the oldest versions that ASB supports) left CI on 2026-10-28, because their package downloads from the AlmaLinux mirror were often very slow: on the release commit of `1.13.0`, the `check alma-9` job stopped at its 30-minute limit, and its rerun was cancelled after 26 minutes. Their last check passed on `b63f9dd`, the source of ASB `1.13.0`. Two rows run on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`; the others run on `ubuntu-latest`. The GTK and Libadwaita values are what the probe measured on 2026-10-20. Gentoo, Solus, and the image-based systems have no job.

| Row | Image | Tier | Node.js | Package | GTK / Libadwaita |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `debian-13` | `debian:13` | Board | Repository | `.deb` | 4.18.6 / 1.7.6 |
| `debian-12` | `debian:12` | Engine | NodeSource | `.deb` | 4.8.3 / 1.2.2 |
| `ubuntu-24.04` | `ubuntu:24.04` | Board | NodeSource | `.deb` | 4.14.5 / 1.5.0 |
| `ubuntu-22.04` | `ubuntu:22.04` | Engine | NodeSource | `.deb` | 4.6.9 / 1.1.7 |
| `ubuntu-26.04` | `ubuntu:26.04` | Board | Repository | `.deb` | 4.22.4 / 1.9.1 |
| `fedora-43` | `fedora:43` | Board | Repository | `.rpm` | 4.20.4 / 1.8.8 |
| `fedora-44` | `fedora:44` | Board | Repository | `.rpm` | 4.22.5 / 1.9.4 |
| `alma-10` | `almalinux:10` | Board, `window: skip` | Repository | `.rpm` | 4.16.7 / 1.6.10 |
| `tumbleweed` | `opensuse/tumbleweed` | Board | Repository | `.rpm` | 4.22.5 / 1.9.4 |
| `leap-16` | `opensuse/leap:16.0` | Board | Repository | `.rpm` | 4.18.6 / 1.7.5 |
| `arch` | `archlinux:latest` | Board | Repository | `PKGBUILD` | 4.24.1 / 1.10.0 |
| `alpine-3.24` | `alpine:3.24` | Board | Repository | none | 4.22.4 / 1.9.2 |
| `void` | `ghcr.io/void-linux/void-glibc-full:latest` | Board | Repository | none | 4.22.4 / 1.9.1 |
| `debian-13-arm` | `debian:13` | Board | Repository | `.deb` | as `debian-13` |
| `fedora-44-arm` | `fedora:44` | Board | Repository | `.rpm` | as `fedora-44` |

- **Tier and Node.js.** A board row needs Python 3.9, PyGObject, GTK 4.12, Libadwaita 1.4, and (unless it has `window: skip`) `xvfb-run` and `dbus-run-session`. The engine rows (Debian 12 and Ubuntu 22.04) have an older GTK and prove that `asb` exits with code 3. The NodeSource rows leave `node` out of the table keys, install `curl` and `ca-certificates`, download `https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_24.x`, run it, and install `nodejs`. The `module` route (`dnf module install -y nodejs:24`) has no row since the `alma-9` rows left. The refresh of the package index is `apt-get update`, `pacman -Syu --noconfirm`, `apk update`, or `xbps-install -Syu xbps`; `dnf` and `zypper` need none.
- **`probe`.** In each image it makes the install line for all the keys of the package table with `node src/linux-distros.mjs <family> <keys>`, refreshes the index, adds Node.js by the route of the row, runs the line, and runs `test/distro-probe.sh <tier> <family>`. The probe prints one JSON object (`ID`, `ID_LIKE`, `VERSION_ID`, the `detect` result, the tool and library versions, cairo support, `qrcode`, and the paths of the sound players, `xvfb-run`, and `dbus-run-session`) and exits with code 1 when the row misses its tier. It uploads `probe.json` as the artifact `distro-probe-<job>`.
- **`build`.** One `ubuntu-latest` job installs `rpm`, runs `npm run build`, and uploads `dist/`.
- **`check`.** Each row runs `docker run --rm -v "$PWD:/asb:ro" ... sh -eu /asb/test/distro-check.sh <family> <tier> <package>` with the install commands in environment variables. The script refuses to run (exit code 2) without `ASB_DISTRO_CHECK=1` or as a user other than root. It installs the packages of the row, makes the user `asbtest` (AlmaLinux 10 has no `su`, so Python drops root there), unpacks the archive, and runs `install.sh`. Then, with `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, the session bus, and the desktop variables unset: `asb doctor --json` (`board.ok` must match the tier and `commands.required` must be empty); the engine start (the runtime file must be in `~/.local/state/asb/run`), `asb agent status`, and the engine stop; `asb service install` with no desktop (exit code 1, the `none` mode, no entry); `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=CI asb service install` (the `xdg` mode, an entry with the ASB marker and the launcher, and a running engine); and `asb service uninstall` (the entry goes and the engine stops). A board row then opens the window with `dbus-run-session -- xvfb-run -a`. Inside it, `test/wait-for-display.py` waits at most 30 s until GDK can open the display, because `xvfb-run` can start its command before Xvfb accepts clients; then the board starts. Xvfb runs with `-noreset`: without it, Xvfb resets when its last set-up client leaves (the engine's GTK check), and the reset drops a board connection that is not set up yet (seen once in CI, on `debian-13`). Xvfb writes its errors to `/tmp/asb-xvfb.log`, and a failed window step prints it. The step waits at most 90 s for the `ASB_SNAPSHOT` image (at least 1 KB, with the same size on two checks). A `snapshot: skip` row waits for `UI: verified` from `asb agent status` instead (no row has it now: `alma-9` had it, because PyGObject 3.40 cannot save the board image). A `window: skip` row (`alma-10`: RHEL 10 has no Xvfb, because the X.Org server was removed and `xwfb-run` replaced `xvfb-run`) skips the window; its doctor step still proves that GTK and Libadwaita load. An engine row checks the exit code 3 and the engine-only message. Next the script runs `test/asb_native_logic_test.py` (and `test/asb_tray_test.py` on a board row) with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, because the repository is mounted read-only, and `uninstall.sh`. At last it installs the release package of the row as root (`apt-get install` for the `.deb`; `dnf install` or `zypper install --allow-unsigned-rpm` for the `.rpm`; `makepkg` as `asbtest` with the archive next to the `PKGBUILD`, then `pacman -U`; `alpine-3.24` and `void` have none) and repeats the doctor and engine steps with `/usr/bin/asb`. On a `preferences: check` row (no row has it now; `alma-9` had it), step 11 then runs `test/preferences-check.py` with the installed board script (`/usr/lib/asb/scripts/asb-native.py`) in Xvfb, with made-up profiles, fake replies, and no engine. Preferences opens at 420 px at its first map, because GTK 4.12 on Xvfb keeps the size of a shown window. The check requires the narrow layout, content that fits in 420 px (GTK 4.12 gives the window 410 px there), and the section list. Then it opens Profiles with **Back** (the five rows and their bridge states, no horizontal scroll, no cut labels) and the dialog of a Claude profile, which is the modal `Adw.Window` fallback on Libadwaita 1.4 (its fields, Escape closes it, and the focus goes back to its row). A `Gtk-CRITICAL` or `Adwaita-CRITICAL` line fails the step, and its JSON line shows the window and content widths. One GTK consistency warning (`GtkBox ... Expect overlapping widgets.`) does not fail it when it names the title box of an `Adw.ActionRow`: Libadwaita 1.4 fails that GTK check in its own rows, and GTK 4.18 with Libadwaita 1.7 does not.
- **`nix`.** On `ubuntu-latest` with `cachix/install-nix-action`: `nix flake check`, `nix build`, `./result/bin/asb doctor --json` (`board.ok` must be true), and the engine start, status, `asb agent status`, and stop, with a temporary home and no runtime folder. The job uploads `flake.lock` as the artifact `flake-lock`, because nix writes a new lock when an input changes.
- **Tests.** `test/distros-workflow.test.mjs` reads `distros.yml` as text (the `check` rows must equal the `probe` rows) and checks the guards of `distro-check.sh`. `test/linux-distros.test.mjs` checks the package table, the CLI, and `test/distro-probe.sh`.

## Origin and credit

ASB is based on [Agent Mission Control `0.6.0`](https://github.com/forxidian/agent-mission-control/releases/tag/v0.6.0) by forxidian. The original [MIT license](LICENSE) and [upstream release history](CHANGELOG.md#upstream-history) remain. The upstream dashboard, its related service code, and its documentation are removed from this repository. The readers keep no dashboard mode.

The token usage logic (`src/usage-codex.mjs`, `src/usage-claude.mjs`, `src/usage-grok.mjs`, `src/usage-pi.mjs`, `src/usage-antigravity.mjs`, `src/usage-pricing.mjs`, `src/usage-aggregation.mjs`, `src/usage-scan-cache.mjs`, `src/usage-rates.mjs`, and the OpenCode tables and dedupe in `src/usage-opencode.mjs`) is a port of [T3 Code](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code) (MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 T3 Tools Inc.). Each file keeps the notice in its header. See [THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md](THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md).
